      kind: proof

      head: environment

      scope: paper
      flags:
        - orphan
      span:
        file: MeasuresinNM.tex
        from: 2218
        to: 2228
      hash: sha256:6a8f36f6eda16b098706a7c1ec291c516173e2adcd56aa0e3a2fe2d539dcd001
      minhash: lBl0mlL+9fzD4RrdbhaMUQHIFxOZr6y/wGxTvFBPUlJImH/nIdpkZh9zpGPQABjrDcTTEsTAfTXxbqgJm3N6Fz3f7SGXmPf6klY95XPMdmwbvltxg/gs1AmT+yjb/txBasn/V40zEY0X6+iiXNyxTcJlqsJtXJIEH1wV0tNup/O0kw90GJx2QrqpRCmA2Az9/dUuQz35npM5xiLxGw2QaFABcLtA8V0kVRWL3maWvfolhkJeNQbmpOfUMT5UVTvrY6xjbcjatf5feWj6T2qg4u+D9fdByV7k9FsAdgfcLkCZoyGk0VVcfIC1gdAMOEeDDM7UiyAA/2DiToFeu1pDeg==
      census: 2610.19088v1#proof-39
      extractor: ax/v0.5.28
---
<!-- defn-1: body_md -->
Suppose \(M\) is a transitive model of set theory. The  \textit{\(M\)-ultrafilter hypothesis}
    states that if \(U\in M\) is a measure in \(M\),
    \(U\) extends to a countably complete filter in \(V\).
<!-- end defn-1 -->
<!-- defn-2: body_md -->
Let \(\mathscr T_X\) denote the \(\Sigma_2\)-theory of \(V\) with parameters in
    \(\textnormal{Ord}\cup \{X\}\). 
    Let \(\mathscr H_X\) denote the structure \((\textnormal{HOD}_X,\mathscr T_X,{\in})\).
    Also let \(\mathscr T = \mathscr T_\emptyset\) and \(\mathscr H = \mathscr H_\emptyset\).
<!-- end defn-2 -->
<!-- prp-1: body_md -->
\label{prp:hod-realization}
    Suppose \(X\) is a set, \(H\) is a countable transitive structure, and \[\rho : H\to \mathscr H_X\] is an elementary embedding. Then there is a countable transitive model \(\bar V\) of \(\textnormal{ZF}\)
    and a set \(\bar X\in \bar V\)
    such that \(H = (\mathscr H_{\bar X})^{\bar V}\).
    Thus \(\rho\) is partial elementary from \((\bar V,\bar X)\) to \((V,X)\).
<!-- end prp-1 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:derived}
    Assume \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\). 
    There is a definable inner model \(M\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}\) with a limit of Woodin cardinals \(\eta\)
    and a generic elementary embedding \[j : V\to D\]
    where \(D\) is a derived model of \(M\) at \(\eta\).
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:ua}
    Assume \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\). Then
    \(\mathscr H\) satisfies weak comparison:
    if \(H_0\) and \(H_1\) are finitely generated
    structures 
    elementarily embeddable into \(\mathscr H\),
    then either \(H_0\in H_1\), \(H_1\in H_0\),
    or there are close embeddings
    \(k_0 : H_0 \to H^*\) and \(k_1 : H_1\to H^*\).
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Let \(M\) and \(\eta\)
    be as in \cref{thm:derived}, and let \(\mathbb R^* = \mathbb R \cap M\).

    Suppose that \(H\in \mathscr H\) is a countable transitive structure that
    is elementarily embeddable into \(\mathscr H\).\footnote{
        Throughout this section, we work in choiceless Morse--Kelley set theory, 
        which allows us to refer to the first-order satisfaction predicate of \(V\).
        With a bit more work, the proofs can be carried out in \(\textnormal{ZF}\).
    } 
    We begin by constructing a 
    close elementary embedding
    \(k : H \to H^*\) where \(H^*\) is \(\mathscr H^{D^*}\) 
    for a model \(D^*\) of the form \(L_\alpha(\mathbb R^*,\Gamma)\)
    with \(\Gamma\) an initial segment of \(\textnormal{Hom}_{<\eta}^{M}\).

    By \cref{prp:hod-realization}, choose a countable transitive model
    \(\bar V\equiv V\) such that \(\mathscr H^{\bar V}=H\).
    Applying \cref{thm:derived} inside \(\bar V\), let
    \(\bar M\subseteq H\), \(\bar\eta\), and the generic elementary embedding
    \(j : \bar V\to D\) be as in that theorem, where
    \(D\) is a derived model of \(\bar M\) at \(\bar\eta\).
    Define \[i : H\to\mathscr H^D\]
    by \(i = j\restriction H\).
    By the observations following \cref{thm:derived}, \(i\) is definable over \(H\).

    Note that \(\bar M\in M\) and by absoluteness,
    \(M\) sees that \(\bar M\) is elementarily embeddable into \(M\).
    Therefore using Neeman-style \(\mathbb R\)-genericity iterations,\footnote{
        Woodin's original construction used Woodin-style genericity iterations,
        which requires establishing certain instances of the unique branch hypothesis.
        Steel suggested replacing them with Neeman-style iterations using \cite{Steel}.
    } we can iterate \((\bar M,\bar\eta)\) 
    to a model \((M^*,\eta^*)\), again elementarily embeddable into \(M\),
    such that \(\mathbb R^* = \mathbb R\cap M\) are the reals of a symmetric collapse of \(M^*\) at
    \(\eta^*\), and if \(D^*\) is the derived model of \(M^*\) at \(\eta^*\) in \({M}^*(\mathbb R^*)\), then
    \(\Gamma = \textnormal{Hom}^{D^*}\) is an initial segment of 
    \(\textnormal{Hom}_{<\eta}^{M}\).

    Let \[\pi : \bar M \to {M}^*\] be the iteration map.
    Then \(\pi\restriction {\mathscr{H}}^D\) is elementary from
    \({\mathscr{H}}^D\) to \({\mathscr{H}}^{D^*}\).
    Set \(H^* = {\mathscr{H}}^{D^*}\). Define an elementary embedding \(k : H \to H^*\)
    by \[k = \pi \circ i\] 

    We claim that \(k\) is close to \(H\). 
    To prove this, fix \(A\in H^*\), and let us show that
    \(k^{-1}[A] \in H\). We have 
    \(\pi^{-1}[A]\in \bar M\) since \(\pi\) is close to \(\bar M\) and \(A\in M^*\).
    Since \(\bar M\subseteq H\), it follows that \(\pi^{-1}[A]\in H\) and hence \(i^{-1}[\pi^{-1}[A]]\in H\) since \(i\) is definable over \(H\). But \(k^{-1}[A] = i^{-1}[\pi^{-1}[A]]\).
    
    We will use that \[D^* = \textnormal{Hull}^{D^*}(k[H]\cup\Lambda)\]
    where \(\Lambda = P(\mathbb R)\cap D^*\).
    This follows from the fact that \(H^* = \textnormal{Hull}^{H^*}(k[H]\cup k(\Theta^H))\),
    which holds because \(k\) is close to \(H\)
    and every uniform measure of \(H\) lies on a cardinal less than
    or equal to \(\Theta^H\).

    We now show that Weak Comparison holds for \({\mathscr{H}}\).
    Fix any two
    finitely generated models \(H_0\) and \(H_1\) that are elementarily embeddable into \({\mathscr{H}}\). We will construct close embeddings \(k_0 : H_0 \to H^*_0\) and \(k_1 : H_1\to H^*_1\)
    such that \(H^*_0\) and \(H_1^*\) are \textit{comparable}: either they are equal or one belongs to the other.

    For \(\ell = 0,1\), the construction above yields
    a close embedding \(k_\ell : H_\ell\to H_\ell^*\) where \(H_\ell^* =  {\mathscr{H}}^{D_\ell^*}\) for
    a model \(D_\ell^*\) satisfying 
    \(\mathbb R\cap D_\ell^* = \mathbb R\cap M\), \(\Gamma_\ell = \textnormal{Hom}^{D_\ell^*}\lhd \textnormal{Hom}_{<\eta}^{M}\),
    and \(D_\ell^* = \textnormal{Hull}^{D_\ell^*}(k_\ell[H_\ell]\cup \Lambda_\ell)\)
    where \(\Lambda_\ell = P(\mathbb R^*)\cap D_\ell^*\).

    We claim that \(D_0^*\) and \(D_1^*\) are comparable. 
    By Wadge determinacy in \(\textnormal{Hom}_{<\eta}^{M}\), \(\Gamma_0\) and \(\Gamma_1\) are comparable pointclasses. It follows from our assumption that \(V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\)
    that \(\Lambda_0\) and \(\Lambda_1\) are comparable as well.
    (If \(\Gamma_0 = \Gamma_1\), this is trivial, and if \(\Gamma_0 \lhd \Gamma_1\),
    then \(\Gamma_0^\#\in D_1^*\), so \(\Lambda_0\lhd \Lambda_1\).)
    By symmetry, it suffices to show that if \(\Lambda_0 \lhd \Lambda_1\), then \(D_0^*\in D_1^*\). 

    Suppose first that every set of reals has a sharp. 
    We will use that \(D_1^*\) is correct about
    sharps, which means that if \(A\in D_1^*\) and \(D_1^*\vDash B = A^\#\), then 
    in fact, \(B = A^\#\). 
    To see this, note that \(M_1^*\) is correct about sharps
    because \(M_1^*\) is elementarily embeddable into \(M\).
    It follows that \(D_1^*\) is correct about
    sharps, since  \(D_1^*\) is an inner model of a forcing extension of \(M_1^*\).

    Since \(D_{0}^* = \textnormal{Hull}^{D_0^*}(k_0[H_0]\cup\Lambda_0)\) and \(H_0\) is finitely generated, \(D_0^*\) is coded by a subset of \(\Lambda_0\) in \(L(\mathbb R^*,\Lambda_0)\). 
    Since \(\Lambda_0^\#\in D_1^*\), \(D_0^*\in D_1^*\). 

    Suppose instead that some set of reals has no sharp. 
    We then have that \(\theta = \Theta^{H_0}\) is regular in \(H_0\). It follows that
    \(k_0(\theta) = \sup k_0[\theta]\) since \(\theta\) carries no uniform measures in \(H_0\) and \(k_0\) is close to \(H_0\). 
    In particular, \(\textnormal{cf}^{M}(k_0(\theta)) = \omega\).
    (We use that \(k_0\in M\), which follows from the fact that \(H_0^*\in M\) 
    and \(H_0\) is finitely
    generated.) Therefore \(D_{0}^* = \textnormal{Hull}^{D_0^*}(\mathbb R^*\cup \mathcal A)\) where 
    \(\mathcal A = \{a_n : n < \omega\}\) 
    is a countable set in \(M\). Letting \(T_n = \text{Th}^{D_0^*}_{\Sigma_n}(\mathbb R^*\cup \{a_0,\dots,a_{n-1}\})\), we have \(\{T_n\}_{n < \omega}\subseteq D_0^*\) and \(\langle T_n\rangle_{n < \omega}\in M\), and hence \(\langle T_n\rangle_{n < \omega}\in D_1^*\).
    Since \(D_0^*\) can be recovered from  \(\langle T_n\rangle_{n < \omega}\), it follows that
    \(D_0^* \in D_1^*\).

    Thus we have shown that \(D_0^*\) and \(D_1^*\) are comparable.
    If \(D_0^*\in D_1^*\), for example, then \(D_0^*\) is \(\textnormal{OD}\) in \(D_1^*\) and hence
    \(H_0^*\in H_1^*\). It follows that \(H_0^*\) and \(H_1^*\) are comparable, and
    this establishes weak comparison. (Note here that if \(H_0^*\in H_1^*\), then \(H_0\in H_1\).)
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:woodin_strong}
    Assume \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\) and \(\delta\) is the largest Suslin. If
    \(\lambda < \Theta\),
    there is a normal measure \(U\) on \(\delta\)
    with \(j_U(\delta) > \lambda\) and
    \(P(\lambda)\cap j_U(\textnormal{HOD}) = P(\lambda)\cap \textnormal{HOD}\).\qed
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:spector}
    Assume \(\textnormal{ZF} + \textnormal{DC} + V = L(X)\) for some set \(X\). 
    Then for any measure \(U\), in a forcing extension,
    there is an inner model \(M\), an elementary embedding \[j : V \to M\]
    and an embedding \(k : \textnormal{Ult}(V,U)\to M\)
    such that \(\textnormal{Ord}\subseteq \textnormal{ran}(k)\) and
    \(k\circ j_U = j\).
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:ua_hod_s}
    Under \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\), 
    \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\) for every \(s \in\textnormal{Ord}^\omega\).
    \begin{proof}
        We first prove the theorem in the case that \(V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\).
        Let \(H\) be a pointwise definable transitive structure that is elementarily embeddable into \(\mathscr H\). 
        
        Fix a countable transitive model \(\bar V\equiv V\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), constructed by type omission, so that
        \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar V}\). 
        Fix \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\cap \bar V\). We will show \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(P = \mathscr H_s^{\bar V}\).

        Fix internal ultrapowers \(e_0 : P\to P_0\) and \(e_1 : P \to P_1\), and let \(s_n = e_n(s)\). For \(n = 0,1\), we imitate the construction from \cref{thm:ua}. That is, construct \(\bar V_n\equiv V\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\) by relativizing \cref{prp:hod-realization} 
        so that
        \[P_n = \mathscr H_{s_n}^{\bar V_n}\]
        Applying \cref{thm:derived} in \(\bar V_n\), fix \(\bar M_n\subseteq \mathscr H^{\bar V_n}\), \(\bar\eta_n\), and an elementary \(j_n : \bar V_n\to D_n\) where \(D_n\) is a derived model of \(\bar M_n\) at \(\bar\eta_n\).
        Let \(t_n =j_n(s_n)\) and define
        \[
            i_n : P_n\to\mathscr H^{D_n}_{t_n}
        \]
        by \(i_n = j_n\restriction P_n\).

        By \cref{thm:derived}, fix \(\gamma_n < \bar\eta_n\) such that \(t_n\) is
        \({<}\gamma_n\)-generic over \(\bar M_n\).
        Let \(\pi_n : \bar M_n\to M_n^*\) be a Neeman-style \(\mathbb R^*\)-genericity iteration above \(\gamma_n\), where \(\mathbb R^* = \mathbb R\cap \textnormal{HOD}\), such that if \(D_n^*\) is the derived model of \(M_n^*\) as computed in \(M_n^*(\mathbb R^*)\),
        then \(D_0^*\) and \(D_1^*\) are comparable.
        Let \(s_n^* = \pi_n[t_n]\), and let
        \[
            \pi_n^+ : \bar M_n[t_n]\to M_n^*[s_n^*]
        \]
        be the unique extension coming from L\'evy--Solovay.
        Finally let \(k_n = \pi_n^+\circ i_n\), so
        \[
            k_n : P_n\to \mathscr H_{s_n^*}^{D_n^*}
        \]
        is a close embedding.

        We first show that \(D_0^* = D_1^*\).
        We have elementary embeddings
        \begin{align*}
            (k_0\circ e_0)\restriction H : H\to \mathscr H^{D_0^*}\\ 
            (k_1\circ e_1)\restriction H : H \to \mathscr H^{D_1^*}
        \end{align*}
        Assuming towards a contradiction that 
        \(D_0^* \in D_1^*\), for example, we would have
        that \(H\) is coded by a real in \(\mathscr H^{D_1^*}\).
        Since \(\textnormal{crit}(k_1\circ e_1) > (2^{\aleph_0})^H\),
        \(H\) and \(\mathscr H^{D_1^*}\) have the same reals,
        and so \(H\) is coded by a real in \(H\), which is a contradiction. This proves that \(D_0^* = D_1^*\),
        so let us denote this model by \(D^*\).

        Since \(H\) is pointwise definable, it follows that
        \(k_0\circ e_0 \restriction H = k_1\circ e_1\restriction H\).
        Therefore
        \(k_0\circ e_0[s] = k_1\circ e_1[s]\),
        and hence \(k_0\circ e_0(s) = k_1\circ e_1(s)\).
        In other words, \(k_0(s_0) = k_1(s_1)\), and so
        \(\mathscr H_{k_0(s_0)}^{D^*} = \mathscr H_{k_1(s_1)}^{D^*}\).
        Now \(k_0\circ e_0\) and \(k_1\circ e_1\) have the same target model, and
        since every element of \(P = \mathscr H_s^{\bar V}\) is definable in \(P\)
        from \(s\), 
        and \(k_0\circ e_0(s) = k_1\circ e_1(s)\), 
        we obtain \(k_0\circ e_0 = k_1\circ e_1\). This yields \(\textnormal{UA}\) for \(e_0\) and \(e_1\), as desired.

        Now assume \(V \neq L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\).
        Let \(\delta\) be the largest Suslin cardinal. 
        We will show that 
        for all \(s\in \Theta^\omega\), \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\). This suffices for the result: if \(t\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\), then since \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) is a \(\Theta\)-cc extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\), there is a set \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}\) such that \(\textnormal{ot}(S) < \Theta\) and \(\textnormal{ran}(t)\subseteq S\), and therefore \(\textnormal{HOD}_t = \textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in \Theta^\omega\).

        Let \(\delta\) be the largest Suslin cardinal, which exists since
        \(\textnormal{Hom}\neq P(\mathbb R)\). 
        By Suslin-co-Suslin reflection, \(\textnormal{Hom} = \textnormal{Hom}^{L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})}\)
        and for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\), \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta = \textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\cap V_\delta \).
        Thus, \(L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\) satisfies \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\),
        so by the previous case, \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\) for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\).
        In the context of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\), since \(\textnormal{UA}\) is a \(\Pi_2\)-sentence, 
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) is downwards absolute to any 
        \(V_\rho\) with \(|V_\rho| = \rho\).
        Thus for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\),
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \[\textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\cap V_\delta = \textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta\]

        Assume towards a contradiction that \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in\Theta^\omega\). 
        Fix a limit ordinal \(\gamma < \Theta\) such that \(\sup(s) < \gamma\) and \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\).
        We claim there is some \(\lambda < \Theta\) large enough that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma \subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\).
        For this, note that every bounded subset of \(\Theta\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has a \(\mathbb B\)-name in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\Theta\) where \(\mathbb B\) is the \(\infty\)-Borel Vop\v{e}nka algebra adding an element of \(\gamma^\omega\) to \(\textnormal{HOD}\). 
        This is by a nice name argument, using that 
        \(\mathbb B\) is \(\Theta\)-cc in \(\textnormal{HOD}\)
        and \(\mathbb B\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\Theta\). 
        Now let \(S\) be a bounded subset of \(\Theta\) coding \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\), and fix \(\lambda < \Theta\) large enough that this set has a \(\mathbb B\)-name \(\dot S\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\). Then
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma \subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\),
        since \(\dot S\) can be interpreted  
        in \((\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\).
       
        Let \(j : V\to M\) be a Spector ultrapower by a \(\delta\)-complete measure on \(\delta\) such that
        \(j(\delta) > \lambda\), \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\cap V_\lambda = \textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\). Note that \(\textnormal{Ord}^\omega\cap V\subseteq M\) by countable choice, and in particular \(s\in M\). 
        Since all measures on ordinals are \(\textnormal{OD}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s \subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_s\). On the other hand,
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\), so \[\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\] 
        It follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma = \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_\gamma\). Therefore \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_\gamma\), and hence it fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_{j(\delta)}\),
        contradicting that in \(V\),
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta\)
        for any \(s\in \delta^\omega\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We first prove the theorem in the case that \(V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\).
        Let \(H\) be a pointwise definable transitive structure that is elementarily embeddable into \(\mathscr H\). 
        
        Fix a countable transitive model \(\bar V\equiv V\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), constructed by type omission, so that
        \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar V}\). 
        Fix \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\cap \bar V\). We will show \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(P = \mathscr H_s^{\bar V}\).

        Fix internal ultrapowers \(e_0 : P\to P_0\) and \(e_1 : P \to P_1\), and let \(s_n = e_n(s)\). For \(n = 0,1\), we imitate the construction from \cref{thm:ua}. That is, construct \(\bar V_n\equiv V\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\) by relativizing \cref{prp:hod-realization} 
        so that
        \[P_n = \mathscr H_{s_n}^{\bar V_n}\]
        Applying \cref{thm:derived} in \(\bar V_n\), fix \(\bar M_n\subseteq \mathscr H^{\bar V_n}\), \(\bar\eta_n\), and an elementary \(j_n : \bar V_n\to D_n\) where \(D_n\) is a derived model of \(\bar M_n\) at \(\bar\eta_n\).
        Let \(t_n =j_n(s_n)\) and define
        \[
            i_n : P_n\to\mathscr H^{D_n}_{t_n}
        \]
        by \(i_n = j_n\restriction P_n\).

        By \cref{thm:derived}, fix \(\gamma_n < \bar\eta_n\) such that \(t_n\) is
        \({<}\gamma_n\)-generic over \(\bar M_n\).
        Let \(\pi_n : \bar M_n\to M_n^*\) be a Neeman-style \(\mathbb R^*\)-genericity iteration above \(\gamma_n\), where \(\mathbb R^* = \mathbb R\cap \textnormal{HOD}\), such that if \(D_n^*\) is the derived model of \(M_n^*\) as computed in \(M_n^*(\mathbb R^*)\),
        then \(D_0^*\) and \(D_1^*\) are comparable.
        Let \(s_n^* = \pi_n[t_n]\), and let
        \[
            \pi_n^+ : \bar M_n[t_n]\to M_n^*[s_n^*]
        \]
        be the unique extension coming from L\'evy--Solovay.
        Finally let \(k_n = \pi_n^+\circ i_n\), so
        \[
            k_n : P_n\to \mathscr H_{s_n^*}^{D_n^*}
        \]
        is a close embedding.

        We first show that \(D_0^* = D_1^*\).
        We have elementary embeddings
        \begin{align*}
            (k_0\circ e_0)\restriction H : H\to \mathscr H^{D_0^*}\\ 
            (k_1\circ e_1)\restriction H : H \to \mathscr H^{D_1^*}
        \end{align*}
        Assuming towards a contradiction that 
        \(D_0^* \in D_1^*\), for example, we would have
        that \(H\) is coded by a real in \(\mathscr H^{D_1^*}\).
        Since \(\textnormal{crit}(k_1\circ e_1) > (2^{\aleph_0})^H\),
        \(H\) and \(\mathscr H^{D_1^*}\) have the same reals,
        and so \(H\) is coded by a real in \(H\), which is a contradiction. This proves that \(D_0^* = D_1^*\),
        so let us denote this model by \(D^*\).

        Since \(H\) is pointwise definable, it follows that
        \(k_0\circ e_0 \restriction H = k_1\circ e_1\restriction H\).
        Therefore
        \(k_0\circ e_0[s] = k_1\circ e_1[s]\),
        and hence \(k_0\circ e_0(s) = k_1\circ e_1(s)\).
        In other words, \(k_0(s_0) = k_1(s_1)\), and so
        \(\mathscr H_{k_0(s_0)}^{D^*} = \mathscr H_{k_1(s_1)}^{D^*}\).
        Now \(k_0\circ e_0\) and \(k_1\circ e_1\) have the same target model, and
        since every element of \(P = \mathscr H_s^{\bar V}\) is definable in \(P\)
        from \(s\), 
        and \(k_0\circ e_0(s) = k_1\circ e_1(s)\), 
        we obtain \(k_0\circ e_0 = k_1\circ e_1\). This yields \(\textnormal{UA}\) for \(e_0\) and \(e_1\), as desired.

        Now assume \(V \neq L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\).
        Let \(\delta\) be the largest Suslin cardinal. 
        We will show that 
        for all \(s\in \Theta^\omega\), \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\). This suffices for the result: if \(t\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\), then since \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) is a \(\Theta\)-cc extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\), there is a set \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}\) such that \(\textnormal{ot}(S) < \Theta\) and \(\textnormal{ran}(t)\subseteq S\), and therefore \(\textnormal{HOD}_t = \textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in \Theta^\omega\).

        Let \(\delta\) be the largest Suslin cardinal, which exists since
        \(\textnormal{Hom}\neq P(\mathbb R)\). 
        By Suslin-co-Suslin reflection, \(\textnormal{Hom} = \textnormal{Hom}^{L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})}\)
        and for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\), \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta = \textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\cap V_\delta \).
        Thus, \(L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\) satisfies \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\),
        so by the previous case, \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\) for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\).
        In the context of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\), since \(\textnormal{UA}\) is a \(\Pi_2\)-sentence, 
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) is downwards absolute to any 
        \(V_\rho\) with \(|V_\rho| = \rho\).
        Thus for all \(s\in \delta^\omega\),
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \[\textnormal{HOD}^{L(\mathbb R, \textnormal{Hom})}_s\cap V_\delta = \textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta\]

        Assume towards a contradiction that \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in\Theta^\omega\). 
        Fix a limit ordinal \(\gamma < \Theta\) such that \(\sup(s) < \gamma\) and \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\).
        We claim there is some \(\lambda < \Theta\) large enough that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma \subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\).
        For this, note that every bounded subset of \(\Theta\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has a \(\mathbb B\)-name in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\Theta\) where \(\mathbb B\) is the \(\infty\)-Borel Vop\v{e}nka algebra adding an element of \(\gamma^\omega\) to \(\textnormal{HOD}\). 
        This is by a nice name argument, using that 
        \(\mathbb B\) is \(\Theta\)-cc in \(\textnormal{HOD}\)
        and \(\mathbb B\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\Theta\). 
        Now let \(S\) be a bounded subset of \(\Theta\) coding \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\), and fix \(\lambda < \Theta\) large enough that this set has a \(\mathbb B\)-name \(\dot S\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\). Then
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma \subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\),
        since \(\dot S\) can be interpreted  
        in \((\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\).
       
        Let \(j : V\to M\) be a Spector ultrapower by a \(\delta\)-complete measure on \(\delta\) such that
        \(j(\delta) > \lambda\), \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\cap V_\lambda = \textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\). Note that \(\textnormal{Ord}^\omega\cap V\subseteq M\) by countable choice, and in particular \(s\in M\). 
        Since all measures on ordinals are \(\textnormal{OD}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s \subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_s\). On the other hand,
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\), so \[\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma\subseteq (\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\lambda)[s]\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\] 
        It follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\gamma = \textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_\gamma\). Therefore \(\textnormal{UA}\) fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_\gamma\), and hence it fails in \(\textnormal{HOD}^M_s\cap V_{j(\delta)}\),
        contradicting that in \(V\),
        \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\delta\)
        for any \(s\in \delta^\omega\).
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:hod_uf}
    Assume \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\). Then for all \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\), the \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)-ultrafilter hypothesis holds.
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
We can reduce to the case that \(V = L(\mathbb R,\textnormal{Hom})\)
    by essentially the same argument from \cref{thm:ua_hod_s}.

    Suppose \(H\) is the pointwise definable hull of \(\mathscr H\).
    By \cref{prp:hod-realization}, we can form a model \(\bar V\equiv V\)
    such that \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar V}\). 
    Fix \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\cap \bar V\), and suppose \(e : H_s \to H_1\) is the ultrapower of \(H_s = (\mathscr H_s)^{\bar V}\) by a measure \(U\in H_s\).
    It suffices to show that
    \(U\) extends to a countably complete filter in \(\bar V\).
    Fix \(\sigma \in (P_{\omega_1}(U))^{\bar V}\). We will show that
    \(\bigcap \sigma\neq \emptyset\).

    We use the construction from the proof of \cref{thm:ua_hod_s},
    with \(M\) and \(\eta\) as in \cref{thm:derived} and
    \(\mathbb R^*=\mathbb R\cap M\).
    Applying \cref{thm:derived} inside \(\bar V\), let
    \(\bar M\subseteq H\), \(\bar\eta\), and the generic elementary embedding
    \(j:\bar V\to D\) be as in that theorem, where
    \(D\) is a derived model of \(\bar M\) at \(\bar\eta\). 
    Since \(j(\sigma)\) and \(j(s)\) are in \(D\), they belong to a common
    \({<}\bar\eta\)-generic extension of \(\bar M\).
    Thus by taking the critical point of the genericity iteration
    \(\pi : \bar M \to M^*\) sufficiently large, we can ensure that
    \(\pi\) lifts uniquely to an elementary embedding
    \(\pi^+\) of
    \(\bar M[j(\sigma),j(s)]\).
    As in \cref{thm:ua_hod_s}, let \(k = \pi^+\circ j\restriction H_s\).

    Let \(Q = \mathscr H_{k(s)}^{D^*}\) be the target model of \(k\).
    The construction from \cref{thm:ua_hod_s} yields \(k_1 : H_1\to Q\) such that
    \(k = k_1\circ e\).
    
    Let \(a = [\textnormal{id}]_U\). Then \(a\in \bigcap e[\sigma]\),
    so \(k_1(a)\in \bigcap (k_1\circ e)[\sigma] =  \bigcap k[\sigma]\). 
    In particular, \(\bigcap k[\sigma]\neq \emptyset\), and 
    since \(k[\sigma] = \pi^+(j[\sigma])\), it follows that
    \(\bigcap j[\sigma]\neq \emptyset\).
    Finally, the elementarity of
    \(j:\bar V\to D\) implies \(\bigcap\sigma\neq\emptyset\).
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- defn-3: body_md -->
Let \(I_\xi = \textstyle\bigcup_{\beta < \xi}\beta^\omega\).
Recall the \textit{Nairian models} from the introduction:
\[N_\xi^+ = \textnormal{HOD}_{I_\xi},\qquad N_\xi = N_\xi^+\cap V_\xi\]
and our convention that \(N^+\) denotes \(N_\xi^+\) and \(N\) denotes \(N_\xi\).
<!-- end defn-3 -->
<!-- defn-4: body_md -->
The \textit{Ketonen order} is defined on \(U,W\in\textnormal{meas}(\delta)\)
    by setting \(U<_{\Bbbk} W\) if for some \(S\in W\),
    there are measures \((U_\alpha)_{\alpha \in S}\in\prod_{\alpha\in S}\textnormal{meas}(\alpha)\)
    such that for all \(A\subseteq \delta\), 
    \[A\in U\iff \{\alpha\in S : A\cap \alpha\in U_\alpha\}\in W\]
<!-- end defn-4 -->
<!-- defn-5: body_md -->
For each cardinal \(\delta\), \(\mathscr K_\delta\)
denotes the Ketonen least uniform measure on \(\delta\), if one exists.
<!-- end defn-5 -->
<!-- lma-1: body_md -->
\label{lma:min_weakly_normal}
    If \(\delta\) is regular and \(\mathscr K_\delta\) exists, then \(\mathscr K_{\delta}\) is the unique weakly normal measure on \(\delta\) concentrating on ordinals \(\alpha\) such that \(\mathscr K_{\textnormal{cf}(\alpha)}\)
    does not exist.\qed
<!-- end lma-1 -->
<!-- lma-2: body_md -->
Assume \(\textnormal{AD} + \textnormal{DC}_\mathbb R\).\label{lma:ad_weakly_normal}
    Every ordinal \(\delta < \Theta\) of uncountable cofinality 
    carries a weakly normal measure that concentrates 
    on \(S^\delta_\omega\).
    \begin{proof}
        Let \(W\) be a Ketonen minimal measure
        extending the tail filter on \(\delta\).
        If \(f : \delta\to \delta\) is regressive on a \(W\)-large set,
        then \(f_*(W) <_{\Bbbk} W\), and so \(f_*(W)\) is not uniform, which means that
        \(f_*(W)\) concentrates on a bounded set; in other words, \(f\) is bounded on a \(W\)-large set.
        This proves \(W\) is weakly normal.

        If \(S^\delta_\omega\notin W\), let \(S = \delta\setminus S^\delta_\omega\). 
        For each \(\alpha\in S\), choose
        \(U_\alpha\in \textnormal{meas}(\alpha)\) extending the tail filter on \(\alpha\).
        (Recall Kunen's theorem that the measures are well-ordered.)
        Put \(A\in U\) if and only if \(\{\alpha\in S : A\cap \alpha\in U_\alpha\}\in W\).
        Then \(U<_{\Bbbk} W\), but clearly \(U\) extends the tail filter, contrary to the minimality
        of \(W\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Let \(W\) be a Ketonen minimal measure
        extending the tail filter on \(\delta\).
        If \(f : \delta\to \delta\) is regressive on a \(W\)-large set,
        then \(f_*(W) <_{\Bbbk} W\), and so \(f_*(W)\) is not uniform, which means that
        \(f_*(W)\) concentrates on a bounded set; in other words, \(f\) is bounded on a \(W\)-large set.
        This proves \(W\) is weakly normal.

        If \(S^\delta_\omega\notin W\), let \(S = \delta\setminus S^\delta_\omega\). 
        For each \(\alpha\in S\), choose
        \(U_\alpha\in \textnormal{meas}(\alpha)\) extending the tail filter on \(\alpha\).
        (Recall Kunen's theorem that the measures are well-ordered.)
        Put \(A\in U\) if and only if \(\{\alpha\in S : A\cap \alpha\in U_\alpha\}\in W\).
        Then \(U<_{\Bbbk} W\), but clearly \(U\) extends the tail filter, contrary to the minimality
        of \(W\).
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- defn-6: body_md -->
For any family of sets \(F\), \(\kappa_F\)
denotes the least cardinal \(\gamma\) such that for some sequence \(\langle A_\xi : \xi < \gamma\rangle\subseteq F\),
\(\bigcap_{\xi < \gamma}A_\xi = \emptyset\).
<!-- end defn-6 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:supercompact}
    Suppose \(\delta\) is a regular cardinal that carries distinct seminormal measures, and let \(\kappa = \kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta}\).
    Then \(\mathscr K_\delta\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact and \({<}\delta\)-supercompact. Moreover, \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact. \qed
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- defn-7: body_md -->
If \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) and \(\eta\) is a cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\),
    then \(\mathscr K_\eta^s\) denotes \((\mathscr K_\eta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\), if \((\mathscr K_\eta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\) exists.
<!-- end defn-7 -->
<!-- lma-3: body_md -->
\label{lma:ketonen}
    Suppose \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\), \(\kappa\) is a cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), and \(\eta\in(\kappa,\Theta)\) is regular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\). If \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\eta\), then \((\kappa_{\mathscr K_\eta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \leq \kappa\).
    \begin{proof}
        The \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\eta\) implies \(\eta\) has uncountable cofinality, and hence \(\mathscr K^s_\eta\) exists. 
        Note that \(S^\eta_\omega \in \mathscr K_\eta^s\) by \cref{lma:min_weakly_normal}: by the ordinal definability of measures, if \(\alpha < \Theta\) and \(\textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\alpha)\) carries no uniform measure in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), then \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\).
        The \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property then implies
        \((S^\eta_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \in \mathscr K_\eta^s\).
        
        Now let \(j : \textnormal{HOD}_s\to M\) be the ultrapower embedding associated to \(\mathscr K_\eta^s\).
        We must show \(\textnormal{crit}(j)\leq \kappa\). By \L o\'s's theorem, \([\textnormal{id}]_{\mathscr K^s_\eta} \in \left(S^{j(\eta)}_{<j(\kappa)}\right)^{M}\), or in other words:
        \[\textnormal{cf}^M(\sup j[\eta]) < j(\kappa)\]
        Since \(\textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\sup j[\eta]) = \eta\),
        we must have \(j(\kappa) \geq \eta\), and hence \(\textnormal{crit}(j)\leq \kappa\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\eta\) implies \(\eta\) has uncountable cofinality, and hence \(\mathscr K^s_\eta\) exists. 
        Note that \(S^\eta_\omega \in \mathscr K_\eta^s\) by \cref{lma:min_weakly_normal}: by the ordinal definability of measures, if \(\alpha < \Theta\) and \(\textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\alpha)\) carries no uniform measure in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), then \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\).
        The \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property then implies
        \((S^\eta_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \in \mathscr K_\eta^s\).
        
        Now let \(j : \textnormal{HOD}_s\to M\) be the ultrapower embedding associated to \(\mathscr K_\eta^s\).
        We must show \(\textnormal{crit}(j)\leq \kappa\). By \L o\'s's theorem, \([\textnormal{id}]_{\mathscr K^s_\eta} \in \left(S^{j(\eta)}_{<j(\kappa)}\right)^{M}\), or in other words:
        \[\textnormal{cf}^M(\sup j[\eta]) < j(\kappa)\]
        Since \(\textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\sup j[\eta]) = \eta\),
        we must have \(j(\kappa) \geq \eta\), and hence \(\textnormal{crit}(j)\leq \kappa\).
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lma-4: body_md -->
\label{lma:seminormal}
    For all sequences \(s\in I_\xi\), \(\delta\) carries distinct seminormal measures
    in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
    \begin{proof}
        Since \(\xi\) is a strong limit cardinal in \(N^+\), 
        \((2^\delta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} < \xi\). 
        In particular, every \(\omega\)-sequence of \(\textnormal{OD}_s\)-subsets of \(\delta\)
        belongs to \(N\).
        Let 
        \(S_0 = S^\delta_\omega\) and \(S_1 = \delta \setminus S_0\);
        since \(\delta\geq \omega_2\), both are stationary in \(N\).
        Let \(F\) be the club filter of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) and let \(F_0 = F|S_0\) and \(F_1 = F|S_1\). 
        Then \(F_0\) and \(F_1\) extend to countably complete filters
        in \(V\), and thus extend to measures \(U_0\) and \(U_1\) in \(V\).
        By Kunen's theorem, these measures are \(\textnormal{OD}\),
        and therefore \(U_0\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) and \(U_1\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) witness
        that in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there are distinct seminormal measures
        on \(\delta\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Since \(\xi\) is a strong limit cardinal in \(N^+\), 
        \((2^\delta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} < \xi\). 
        In particular, every \(\omega\)-sequence of \(\textnormal{OD}_s\)-subsets of \(\delta\)
        belongs to \(N\).
        Let 
        \(S_0 = S^\delta_\omega\) and \(S_1 = \delta \setminus S_0\);
        since \(\delta\geq \omega_2\), both are stationary in \(N\).
        Let \(F\) be the club filter of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) and let \(F_0 = F|S_0\) and \(F_1 = F|S_1\). 
        Then \(F_0\) and \(F_1\) extend to countably complete filters
        in \(V\), and thus extend to measures \(U_0\) and \(U_1\) in \(V\).
        By Kunen's theorem, these measures are \(\textnormal{OD}\),
        and therefore \(U_0\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) and \(U_1\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) witness
        that in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there are distinct seminormal measures
        on \(\delta\).
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- prp-2: body_md -->
\label{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}
    Suppose \(s\in I_\xi\), 
    \(\bar \kappa = (\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta^s})^{N}\), and
    \(\kappa = (\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta^s})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\).
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\bar \kappa,\kappa)\)-cover property at
         \(\delta\) in \(N\).
        \item \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact
        and \({<}\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        \item \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
    \end{enumerate}
    \begin{proof}
        Since \(\delta\) carries distinct seminormal measures, 
        we can apply \cref{thm:supercompact} 
        to conclude that \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact
        and \({<}\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\),
        and moreover \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Since \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there is a function \(p : \delta\to P_\kappa(\delta)\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        such that \(\mathcal U = p_*(\mathscr K_\delta^s)\) is a fine measure on \(P_\kappa(\delta)\).

        The \((\bar \kappa,\kappa)\)-cover property now follows.
        Fix \(\sigma \in P_{\bar \kappa}(\delta)\cap N\).
        For \(\alpha < \delta\), define
        \[A_\alpha = \left\{\tau\in \left(P_\kappa(\delta)\right)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} : \alpha\in \tau\right\}\]
        so \(A_\alpha\in \mathcal U\) by fineness.
        Since \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) is \(\bar \kappa\)-complete,
        \(\bigcap_{\alpha\in \sigma}A_\alpha\neq \emptyset\),
        and if \(\tau\) belongs to this intersection, then \(\tau\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\), 
        \(\sigma\subseteq \tau\)
        and \(|\tau|^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} < \kappa\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Since \(\delta\) carries distinct seminormal measures, 
        we can apply \cref{thm:supercompact} 
        to conclude that \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact
        and \({<}\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\),
        and moreover \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Since \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) witnesses that \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there is a function \(p : \delta\to P_\kappa(\delta)\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        such that \(\mathcal U = p_*(\mathscr K_\delta^s)\) is a fine measure on \(P_\kappa(\delta)\).

        The \((\bar \kappa,\kappa)\)-cover property now follows.
        Fix \(\sigma \in P_{\bar \kappa}(\delta)\cap N\).
        For \(\alpha < \delta\), define
        \[A_\alpha = \left\{\tau\in \left(P_\kappa(\delta)\right)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} : \alpha\in \tau\right\}\]
        so \(A_\alpha\in \mathcal U\) by fineness.
        Since \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) is \(\bar \kappa\)-complete,
        \(\bigcap_{\alpha\in \sigma}A_\alpha\neq \emptyset\),
        and if \(\tau\) belongs to this intersection, then \(\tau\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\), 
        \(\sigma\subseteq \tau\)
        and \(|\tau|^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} < \kappa\).
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- prp-3: body_md -->
\label{prp:ks_complete}
    For any \(s\in I_\xi\), 
    \(\mathscr K_\delta^s\) is \(\Theta^{N}\)-complete in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
    \begin{proof}
        Otherwise, by \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov},
        for some \(\kappa < \Theta^{N}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\delta\).
        To get a contradiction, fix \(\gamma \in (\kappa,\Theta^{N})\)
        such that \(\gamma\to (\gamma)^{<\omega_1}\). 
        By a standard argument, there is an \(\omega\)-club of \(\alpha < \gamma\) such that
        \(\alpha\) is regular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).\footnote{Let \(U\) be
        the \(\omega\)-club filter on \(\gamma\). The partition property
        implies that \(U\) is a normal measure,
        and hence \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is a normal measure in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies \(\textnormal{ZFC}\), \(\textnormal{Reg}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\cap \gamma\in U\).}
        This contradicts that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the 
         \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\gamma\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Otherwise, by \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov},
        for some \(\kappa < \Theta^{N}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\delta\).
        To get a contradiction, fix \(\gamma \in (\kappa,\Theta^{N})\)
        such that \(\gamma\to (\gamma)^{<\omega_1}\). 
        By a standard argument, there is an \(\omega\)-club of \(\alpha < \gamma\) such that
        \(\alpha\) is regular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).\footnote{Let \(U\) be
        the \(\omega\)-club filter on \(\gamma\). The partition property
        implies that \(U\) is a normal measure,
        and hence \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is a normal measure in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies \(\textnormal{ZFC}\), \(\textnormal{Reg}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\cap \gamma\in U\).}
        This contradicts that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the 
         \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\gamma\).
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- prp-4: body_md -->
\label{prp:W_to_K}
    Suppose that in \(N\), \(W\) is a weakly normal measure
    on \(\delta\) that concentrates on \(S^\delta_\omega\).
    Then for all \(s\in I_\xi\),
    \(W\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s = \mathscr K^s_\delta\).
    \begin{proof}
        This follows immediately from \cref{lma:min_weakly_normal}
        since \(W\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is weakly normal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and by the \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)-ultrafilter
        hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), \(W\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) 
        concentrates on ordinals \(\alpha\) such that \(\mathscr K_{\bar \alpha}^s\)
        does not exist where \(\bar \alpha = \textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\alpha)\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-4 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
This follows immediately from \cref{lma:min_weakly_normal}
        since \(W\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is weakly normal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and by the \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)-ultrafilter
        hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), \(W\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) 
        concentrates on ordinals \(\alpha\) such that \(\mathscr K_{\bar \alpha}^s\)
        does not exist where \(\bar \alpha = \textnormal{cf}^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}(\alpha)\).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{prp:wd_generation}
    Suppose that in \(N\), \(W\) is a weakly normal measure
    on \(\delta\) that concentrates on \(S^\delta_\omega\).
    Then \(W = \bigcup_{s\in I_\xi}\mathscr K^s_\delta\)
    and \(W\) is \(\Theta^{N}\)-complete.
    \begin{proof}
        This follows from \cref{prp:ks_complete} and \cref{prp:W_to_K} 
        since every subset of \(\delta\) in \(N\) belongs
        to \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in I_\xi\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
This follows from \cref{prp:ks_complete} and \cref{prp:W_to_K} 
        since every subset of \(\delta\) in \(N\) belongs
        to \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for some \(s\in I_\xi\).
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:unique_complete}
    In \(N\), there is a unique weakly 
    normal measure concentrating on \(S^\delta_\omega\).
    \begin{proof}
        Existence follows from \cref{lma:ad_weakly_normal}:
        since \(\delta\) is regular in \(N\),
        \(\textnormal{cf}(\delta) > \omega\); therefore \cref{lma:ad_weakly_normal} provides a weakly normal measure \(W\) on \(\delta\) concentrating on \(S^\delta_\omega\); since \(W\) is \(\textnormal{OD}\), \(W\cap N\in N\).
        Uniqueness is immediate from \cref{prp:wd_generation}.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Existence follows from \cref{lma:ad_weakly_normal}:
        since \(\delta\) is regular in \(N\),
        \(\textnormal{cf}(\delta) > \omega\); therefore \cref{lma:ad_weakly_normal} provides a weakly normal measure \(W\) on \(\delta\) concentrating on \(S^\delta_\omega\); since \(W\) is \(\textnormal{OD}\), \(W\cap N\in N\).
        Uniqueness is immediate from \cref{prp:wd_generation}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- defn-8: body_md -->
\label{defn:w_delta}
    Let \(W_\delta\) denote the unique weakly normal measure of \(N\)  with \(S^\delta_\omega\in W_\delta\),
    and let \(\kappa_\delta\) denote the completeness of \(W_\delta\) as computed in \(N\).
<!-- end defn-8 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:ua_completeness}
    \(\kappa_\delta = \min\left\{(\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta^s})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}:s\in I_\xi\right\}\).\qed
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- prp-5: body_md -->
\label{prp:anti_inner}
    Assume the following holds in \(N\): for all cardinals \(\lambda\), there is no inner model \(M\) of \(\textnormal{ZFC} + \textnormal{UA}\) in which some \(\kappa < \lambda\) is \(\lambda\)-supercompact and such that the \(M\)-ultrafilter hypothesis holds.
    Then in \(N\), every regular cardinal is measurable.
    \begin{proof}
        This follows from \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} and \cref{cor:ua_completeness}.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-5 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
This follows from \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} and \cref{cor:ua_completeness}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- qst-1: body_md -->
Which of the following large cardinal hypotheses has higher consistency strength over ZFC? 
    \begin{itemize}
        \item There is a cardinal \(\kappa\) that is \(\kappa^+\)-supercompact.
        \item There is a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end qst-1 -->
<!-- prp-6: body_md -->
\label{prp:anti_inner2}
    Assume that in \(N\) there is no inner model of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\) containing a cardinal \(\kappa\) that is \(2^{2^\kappa}\)-supercompact.
    Then in \(N\), every regular cardinal \(\delta\) carries a measure that is \(\lambda\)-complete for all cardinals \(\lambda < \delta\).
    \begin{proof}
        The proof is the same as that of \cref{prp:anti_inner}
        using the fact that under \(\textnormal{UA}\), if \(\kappa\) is \(\kappa^{++}\)-supercompact, then \(2^{2^\kappa} = \kappa^{++}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-6 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
The proof is the same as that of \cref{prp:anti_inner}
        using the fact that under \(\textnormal{UA}\), if \(\kappa\) is \(\kappa^{++}\)-supercompact, then \(2^{2^\kappa} = \kappa^{++}\).
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- prp-7: body_md -->
\label{prp:kappa_cov}
    Let \(\kappa = \kappa_\delta\), and suppose \(s\in I_\xi\) is such that \((\kappa_{\mathscr K^s_\delta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = \kappa\). Then
    \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa\)-cover and approximation properties at \(\delta\) in \(N\). Moreover, 
    every \(S\in P(\delta)\cap N\) is in
    \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\).
    \begin{proof}
        The \(\kappa\)-cover property
        follows immediately from \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}.
        For the approximation property, 
        fix \(\mathcal A = \langle A_\alpha : \alpha < \delta\rangle\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\) such that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies that \(\mathcal A\) is a \(\kappa\)-independent family 
        of subsets of \(\delta\). 
        Suppose \(T\in P(\delta)\cap N\) is \(\kappa\)-approximated by \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Then the basis
        \[\mathcal B = \{A_\alpha : \alpha \in T\} \cup \{\delta\setminus A_\alpha : \alpha\notin T\}\]
        extends to a 
        \(\kappa\)-complete filter \(F\) in \(N\). 
        In particular, \(F\) 
        is countably complete in \(V\), and thus \(F\) extends to a measure \(U\) in \(V\).
        By Kunen's theorem, \(U\) is \(\textnormal{OD}\), and so \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Thus
        \(T = \{\alpha < \delta : A_\alpha\in U\}\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\).

        To show every \(S\in P(\delta)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\),
        fix \(t\in I_\xi\) such that \(s\) and \(S\) belong to \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        Since \(\mathscr K^s_\delta\subseteq \mathscr K^t_\delta\),
        \((\kappa_{\mathscr K^t_\delta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_t} = \kappa\).
        In particular, \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\). Let \(x\subseteq \kappa\) code a well-order of \(P_\kappa(\kappa)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\). 
        Note that the models \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) and \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) compute \(P_\kappa(\delta)\) the same way:
        of course, \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_t\), and in the other direction,
        if \(\sigma\in (P_\kappa(\delta))^{\textnormal{HOD}_t}\), then \(\sigma\) is covered by \(\tau\in (P_\kappa(\delta))^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\) by the \(\kappa\)-cover property, \(P(\tau)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) since \(P_\kappa(\kappa)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\). The same argument shows that
        \[(\delta^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}} \leq (\delta^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\cdot (\kappa^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}} = \delta\cdot \kappa = \delta\]
        
        This cardinal arithmetic yields \(\mathcal A = \langle A_\alpha : \alpha < \delta\rangle\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) such that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) satisfies that \(\mathcal A\) is a \(\kappa\)-independent family 
        of subsets of \(\delta\). Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) and \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) 
        have the same \(P_\kappa(\delta)\),
        \(\mathcal A\) is also \(\kappa\)-independent in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, 
        \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        Repeating the argument of the previous paragraph, we can find a \(\kappa\)-complete measure
        \(U\) of \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) such that 
        \(S = \{\alpha < \delta : A_\alpha\in U\}\), and using the \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\)-ultrafilter hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), we obtain that \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\), and therefore \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-7 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The \(\kappa\)-cover property
        follows immediately from \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}.
        For the approximation property, 
        fix \(\mathcal A = \langle A_\alpha : \alpha < \delta\rangle\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\) such that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) satisfies that \(\mathcal A\) is a \(\kappa\)-independent family 
        of subsets of \(\delta\). 
        Suppose \(T\in P(\delta)\cap N\) is \(\kappa\)-approximated by \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Then the basis
        \[\mathcal B = \{A_\alpha : \alpha \in T\} \cup \{\delta\setminus A_\alpha : \alpha\notin T\}\]
        extends to a 
        \(\kappa\)-complete filter \(F\) in \(N\). 
        In particular, \(F\) 
        is countably complete in \(V\), and thus \(F\) extends to a measure \(U\) in \(V\).
        By Kunen's theorem, \(U\) is \(\textnormal{OD}\), and so \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        Thus
        \(T = \{\alpha < \delta : A_\alpha\in U\}\in \textnormal{HOD}_s\).

        To show every \(S\in P(\delta)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\),
        fix \(t\in I_\xi\) such that \(s\) and \(S\) belong to \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        Since \(\mathscr K^s_\delta\subseteq \mathscr K^t_\delta\),
        \((\kappa_{\mathscr K^t_\delta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_t} = \kappa\).
        In particular, \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\). Let \(x\subseteq \kappa\) code a well-order of \(P_\kappa(\kappa)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\). 
        Note that the models \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) and \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) compute \(P_\kappa(\delta)\) the same way:
        of course, \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_t\), and in the other direction,
        if \(\sigma\in (P_\kappa(\delta))^{\textnormal{HOD}_t}\), then \(\sigma\) is covered by \(\tau\in (P_\kappa(\delta))^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\) by the \(\kappa\)-cover property, \(P(\tau)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) since \(P_\kappa(\kappa)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_t\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\). The same argument shows that
        \[(\delta^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}} \leq (\delta^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s}\cdot (\kappa^{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}} = \delta\cdot \kappa = \delta\]
        
        This cardinal arithmetic yields \(\mathcal A = \langle A_\alpha : \alpha < \delta\rangle\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) such that
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) satisfies that \(\mathcal A\) is a \(\kappa\)-independent family 
        of subsets of \(\delta\). Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) and \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) 
        have the same \(P_\kappa(\delta)\),
        \(\mathcal A\) is also \(\kappa\)-independent in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, 
        \(\kappa\) is \(\delta\)-strongly compact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\).
        Repeating the argument of the previous paragraph, we can find a \(\kappa\)-complete measure
        \(U\) of \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\) such that 
        \(S = \{\alpha < \delta : A_\alpha\in U\}\), and using the \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\)-ultrafilter hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), we obtain that \(U\cap \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\), and therefore \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\).
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:monotonicity}
   If \(\delta_0 \leq \delta_1 < \xi\) are regular cardinals in \(N\), then \(\kappa_{\delta_0}\leq \kappa_{\delta_1}\).
    \begin{proof}
        By the Steel--Woodin theorem \cite[Theorem 2.18]{SteelOrdinalDefinability}, we may assume \(\delta_0 \geq \Theta^{N}\).
        By \cref{cor:ua_completeness},
        it suffices to show that for all \(s\in I_\xi\),
        \(\mathscr K_{\delta_1}^s\) is \(\kappa\)-complete in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) where
        \(\kappa = \kappa_{\delta_0}\). 

        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\). A consequence of \cite[Lemma 7.4.49]{UABook} is that under \(\textnormal{ZFC} + \textnormal{UA}\), if \(\gamma_0 \leq \gamma_1\) are regular non-isolated Fr\'echet cardinals, then \(\kappa_{\mathscr K_{\gamma_0}}\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K_{\gamma_1}}\).
        By \cref{lma:seminormal},
        both \(\delta_0\) and \(\delta_1\) carry distinct 
        seminormal measures in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), and so
        by \cite[Theorem 8.4.20]{UABook}, 
        they are regular non-isolated Fr\'echet cardinals in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        By \cref{prp:W_to_K}, it follows that
        \(\kappa \leq \kappa_{\mathscr K^s_{\delta_0}}\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K^s_{\delta_1}}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By the Steel--Woodin theorem \cite[Theorem 2.18]{SteelOrdinalDefinability}, we may assume \(\delta_0 \geq \Theta^{N}\).
        By \cref{cor:ua_completeness},
        it suffices to show that for all \(s\in I_\xi\),
        \(\mathscr K_{\delta_1}^s\) is \(\kappa\)-complete in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) where
        \(\kappa = \kappa_{\delta_0}\). 

        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\). A consequence of \cite[Lemma 7.4.49]{UABook} is that under \(\textnormal{ZFC} + \textnormal{UA}\), if \(\gamma_0 \leq \gamma_1\) are regular non-isolated Fr\'echet cardinals, then \(\kappa_{\mathscr K_{\gamma_0}}\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K_{\gamma_1}}\).
        By \cref{lma:seminormal},
        both \(\delta_0\) and \(\delta_1\) carry distinct 
        seminormal measures in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), and so
        by \cite[Theorem 8.4.20]{UABook}, 
        they are regular non-isolated Fr\'echet cardinals in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).
        By \cref{prp:W_to_K}, it follows that
        \(\kappa \leq \kappa_{\mathscr K^s_{\delta_0}}\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K^s_{\delta_1}}\).
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- thm-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:main}
    Let \(\mathcal H = V_\xi^\textnormal{HOD}\). Inside \(N\), suppose \(\eta\) is a regular cardinal satisfying: 
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \label{item:s_reflection} 
        For all \(\alpha < \eta\), \(P(\alpha) \subseteq \mathcal H({\eta^\omega})\). 
        \item \label{item:small_gen}  For all \(\alpha < \eta\), \(\Theta(\alpha^\omega)\leq \eta\).
    \end{enumerate}
    Then in \(N\), either \(\kappa_\eta = \eta\) or \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\eta^{++}\).
    \begin{proof}
        By \ref{item:small_gen}, \(\Theta^{N} \leq \eta < \xi\),
        which puts us in a position to apply the previous results of this section 
        with \(\delta = \eta\).

        Assume towards a contradiction that the conclusion fails. 
        In other words, \(\kappa < \eta\) where \(\kappa = \kappa_\eta\).
        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\) such that \((\kappa_{\mathscr K_\eta^s})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = \kappa\). 

        By \cref{prp:kappa_cov},
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the 
        \(\kappa\)-cover property at \(\delta\) in \(N\) and every \(S\in P(\eta)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\).
        Combining \ref{item:s_reflection} and \ref{item:small_gen}, for any \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) is an \(\eta\)-cc generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\). Since every
        subset of \(\eta\) in \(N\) belongs to some such \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\), it follows that
        \((\eta^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\eta^+)^{N}\).

        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has
        the \(\kappa\)-cover property at \(\eta\) and \((\eta^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\eta^+)^{N}\), 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa\)-cover property at \((\eta^+)^{N}\).
        Let \(\gamma = (\eta^+)^{N}\).
        Since \(\gamma\) is a cardinal in \(N\) that is 
        seen to be a successor cardinal by an inner model of choice,
        \(\gamma\) is regular in \(N\). 
        Applying \cref{lma:ketonen} and \cref{thm:monotonicity},
        \[\kappa = (\kappa_{\mathscr K^s_\eta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \leq (\kappa_{\mathscr K^s_\gamma})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \leq \kappa\] 
        Thus we can apply \cref{prp:kappa_cov}
        again, to obtain that every \(S\in P(\gamma)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\), and thus
        \((\gamma^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\gamma^+)^{N}\).

        Iterating this argument, we have that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) correctly computes \((\eta^{+n})^{N}\)
        for all \(n < \omega\).
        Let \(\lambda = (\eta^{+\omega})^{N}\). 
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, \(\kappa\) is \({<}\lambda\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) and
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa\)-cover and approximation properties
        below \(\lambda\). Indeed, this holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}\) for all \(u\in I_\xi\).

        We claim that \(\Theta^{N}(P(\eta)) \leq (\gamma^+)^{N}\).
        Note first that since UA implies GCH in regions of supercompactness, 
        \((2^\eta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}} = \gamma\) for all \(u\in I_\xi\).
        For each \(u\in \eta^\omega\), 
        let \(\langle S_\alpha^u : \alpha < \gamma\rangle\) be an \(\textnormal{OD}_{s,u}\)-enumeration of 
        \(P(\eta)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}\). 
        Note that each \(S\subseteq \eta\) is of the form \(S_\alpha^u\) for some \(u\in \eta^\omega\)
        and \(\alpha < \gamma\),
        since \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\) and
        \(x\in \textnormal{HOD}_u\) for some \(u\in \eta^\omega\) by \ref{item:s_reflection}.

        Suppose \(\nu < \Theta^{N}(P(\eta))\),
        and let us show \(\nu < (\gamma^+)^{N}\). 
        Suppose \(f : P(\eta)\to \nu\) is a surjection.
        Define \(g : \eta^\omega\times \gamma\to \nu\) by
        setting \(g(u,\alpha) = f(S^u_\alpha)\). Then \(g\) is also a surjection.
        Note however that for each fixed \(\alpha < \gamma\),
        if \(A_\alpha = \{g(u,\alpha) : u\in \eta^\omega\}\), then
        \(|A_\alpha|\leq \eta\) by \ref{item:small_gen}.
        (Here we use that \(\Theta^{N}(\eta^\omega) = ({\eta^+})^{N}\) as a consequence
        of \ref{item:small_gen}.)
        Since \(\nu = \bigcup_{\alpha < \gamma} A_\alpha\),
        \(\nu < (\gamma^+)^{N}\). 

        Fix \(y\subseteq \eta\) such that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap P_\kappa(\eta)\) is
        coded by \(y\). 
        Since \(\Theta^{N}(P(\eta)) \leq (\gamma^+)^{N}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_y\) is contained in a \((\gamma^+)^{N}\)-cc extension of
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\). Moreover, applying the Hamkins uniqueness theorem
        \cite[Theorem 4.7]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD} at each finite successor of
        \(\eta\), we see that \(Y = P_\textnormal{bd}(\lambda)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is \(\textnormal{OD}_y\).
        In particular, \(\textnormal{HOD}_y^{L(Y)}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y\). 
        Note that \(L(Y)\) is a model of \(\textnormal{ZFC} + \textnormal{UA}\) in which 
        \(\kappa\) is \({<}\lambda\)-supercompact; the fact that
        \(L(Y)\vDash \textnormal{AC}\) follows from \cite[Theorem 6.2.19]{UABook} applied in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), which implies that there is a well-order of \(Y\) that is definable from parameters over \(Y\).
        By \cite[Theorem 6.2.19]{UABook}, it follows that 
        \(Y\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y^{L(Y)}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y\).
        This implies that \(Y\) is contained in a \((\gamma^+)^{N}\)-cc extension
        of \(\textnormal{HOD}\). It follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) correctly computes all 
        finite successors of \((\gamma^+)^{N} = (\eta^{++})^{N}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-9 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
By \ref{item:small_gen}, \(\Theta^{N} \leq \eta < \xi\),
        which puts us in a position to apply the previous results of this section 
        with \(\delta = \eta\).

        Assume towards a contradiction that the conclusion fails. 
        In other words, \(\kappa < \eta\) where \(\kappa = \kappa_\eta\).
        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\) such that \((\kappa_{\mathscr K_\eta^s})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = \kappa\). 

        By \cref{prp:kappa_cov},
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the 
        \(\kappa\)-cover property at \(\delta\) in \(N\) and every \(S\in P(\eta)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\).
        Combining \ref{item:s_reflection} and \ref{item:small_gen}, for any \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) is an \(\eta\)-cc generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\). Since every
        subset of \(\eta\) in \(N\) belongs to some such \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\), it follows that
        \((\eta^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\eta^+)^{N}\).

        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has
        the \(\kappa\)-cover property at \(\eta\) and \((\eta^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\eta^+)^{N}\), 
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa\)-cover property at \((\eta^+)^{N}\).
        Let \(\gamma = (\eta^+)^{N}\).
        Since \(\gamma\) is a cardinal in \(N\) that is 
        seen to be a successor cardinal by an inner model of choice,
        \(\gamma\) is regular in \(N\). 
        Applying \cref{lma:ketonen} and \cref{thm:monotonicity},
        \[\kappa = (\kappa_{\mathscr K^s_\eta})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \leq (\kappa_{\mathscr K^s_\gamma})^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} \leq \kappa\] 
        Thus we can apply \cref{prp:kappa_cov}
        again, to obtain that every \(S\in P(\gamma)\cap N\) is in
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\), and thus
        \((\gamma^+)^{\textnormal{HOD}_s} = (\gamma^+)^{N}\).

        Iterating this argument, we have that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) correctly computes \((\eta^{+n})^{N}\)
        for all \(n < \omega\).
        Let \(\lambda = (\eta^{+\omega})^{N}\). 
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, \(\kappa\) is \({<}\lambda\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) and
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa\)-cover and approximation properties
        below \(\lambda\). Indeed, this holds in \(\textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}\) for all \(u\in I_\xi\).

        We claim that \(\Theta^{N}(P(\eta)) \leq (\gamma^+)^{N}\).
        Note first that since UA implies GCH in regions of supercompactness, 
        \((2^\eta)^{\textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}} = \gamma\) for all \(u\in I_\xi\).
        For each \(u\in \eta^\omega\), 
        let \(\langle S_\alpha^u : \alpha < \gamma\rangle\) be an \(\textnormal{OD}_{s,u}\)-enumeration of 
        \(P(\eta)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_{s,u}\). 
        Note that each \(S\subseteq \eta\) is of the form \(S_\alpha^u\) for some \(u\in \eta^\omega\)
        and \(\alpha < \gamma\),
        since \(S\in \textnormal{HOD}_{s,x}\) for some \(x\in P(\kappa)\cap N\) and
        \(x\in \textnormal{HOD}_u\) for some \(u\in \eta^\omega\) by \ref{item:s_reflection}.

        Suppose \(\nu < \Theta^{N}(P(\eta))\),
        and let us show \(\nu < (\gamma^+)^{N}\). 
        Suppose \(f : P(\eta)\to \nu\) is a surjection.
        Define \(g : \eta^\omega\times \gamma\to \nu\) by
        setting \(g(u,\alpha) = f(S^u_\alpha)\). Then \(g\) is also a surjection.
        Note however that for each fixed \(\alpha < \gamma\),
        if \(A_\alpha = \{g(u,\alpha) : u\in \eta^\omega\}\), then
        \(|A_\alpha|\leq \eta\) by \ref{item:small_gen}.
        (Here we use that \(\Theta^{N}(\eta^\omega) = ({\eta^+})^{N}\) as a consequence
        of \ref{item:small_gen}.)
        Since \(\nu = \bigcup_{\alpha < \gamma} A_\alpha\),
        \(\nu < (\gamma^+)^{N}\). 

        Fix \(y\subseteq \eta\) such that \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap P_\kappa(\eta)\) is
        coded by \(y\). 
        Since \(\Theta^{N}(P(\eta)) \leq (\gamma^+)^{N}\),
        \(\textnormal{HOD}_y\) is contained in a \((\gamma^+)^{N}\)-cc extension of
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\). Moreover, applying the Hamkins uniqueness theorem
        \cite[Theorem 4.7]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD} at each finite successor of
        \(\eta\), we see that \(Y = P_\textnormal{bd}(\lambda)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\) is \(\textnormal{OD}_y\).
        In particular, \(\textnormal{HOD}_y^{L(Y)}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y\). 
        Note that \(L(Y)\) is a model of \(\textnormal{ZFC} + \textnormal{UA}\) in which 
        \(\kappa\) is \({<}\lambda\)-supercompact; the fact that
        \(L(Y)\vDash \textnormal{AC}\) follows from \cite[Theorem 6.2.19]{UABook} applied in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), which implies that there is a well-order of \(Y\) that is definable from parameters over \(Y\).
        By \cite[Theorem 6.2.19]{UABook}, it follows that 
        \(Y\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y^{L(Y)}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_y\).
        This implies that \(Y\) is contained in a \((\gamma^+)^{N}\)-cc extension
        of \(\textnormal{HOD}\). It follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) correctly computes all 
        finite successors of \((\gamma^+)^{N} = (\eta^{++})^{N}\).
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lma-5: body_md -->
\label{lma:power_correct}
    In \(N\), if \(\alpha\geq \omega\)
    and \(\eta = \Theta(V_{\alpha+1})\),
    then for all \(\beta < \eta\), \(P(\beta) \subseteq \mathcal H(\eta^\omega)\).
    \begin{proof}
        If \(\alpha = \omega\), the lemma follows from
        \(\textnormal{AD}_\mathbb R + \textnormal{DC}\). 
        Assume \(\alpha > \omega\).
        Fix \(\beta\) such that \(\Theta \leq \beta < \eta\), and let \(\bar \eta\) be the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\beta\). By \cite[Theorem 19.1]{FailureSquare},
        \(\bar \eta = \Theta(\beta^\omega)\leq \eta\). By \cite[Theorem 10.13]{NairianModels},
        \(P(\beta)\subseteq L(\mathcal H|\bar \eta,I_{\bar \eta})\subseteq \mathcal H(\eta^\omega)\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-5 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
If \(\alpha = \omega\), the lemma follows from
        \(\textnormal{AD}_\mathbb R + \textnormal{DC}\). 
        Assume \(\alpha > \omega\).
        Fix \(\beta\) such that \(\Theta \leq \beta < \eta\), and let \(\bar \eta\) be the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\beta\). By \cite[Theorem 19.1]{FailureSquare},
        \(\bar \eta = \Theta(\beta^\omega)\leq \eta\). By \cite[Theorem 10.13]{NairianModels},
        \(P(\beta)\subseteq L(\mathcal H|\bar \eta,I_{\bar \eta})\subseteq \mathcal H(\eta^\omega)\).
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lma-6: body_md -->
\label{lma:V_vs_I}
    In \(N\), for all \(\alpha\geq \omega\),
    \(|V_{\alpha}| =^* |I_\lambda|\)
    where \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\).
    \begin{proof}
        Work in \(N\). The nontrivial direction is
        \(|V_\alpha| \leq^* |I_\lambda|\).
        We produce the required surjections uniformly in \(\alpha\) by induction. 
        This uniformity handles the limit steps.
        For the successor step, 
        suppose \(|V_{\alpha}| \leq^* |I_\lambda|\), and let us show that \(|V_{\alpha+1}| \leq^* |\Theta(V_\alpha)^\omega|\).

        First, assume \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\).
        Then \(|V_{\alpha+1}| =^* |V_\alpha^\omega| \leq^* |(I_\lambda)^\omega| =^* |\lambda^\omega|\leq^* |\Theta(V_\alpha)^\omega|\). 
        
        Otherwise, let \(\theta = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\), and note that \(\theta \leq \Theta(V_\alpha)\) since \(|(V_\alpha)^\omega| = |V_\alpha|\).
        We use that 
        \(P(\lambda^\omega)\subseteq L_\theta(\mathcal H|\theta,I_\theta)\) by
        \cite[Theorems 10.6 and 10.13]{NairianModels}.
        Since every subset of \(V_\alpha\) can be coded by a subset of \(I_\lambda\),
        it follows that \(V_{\alpha+1}\subseteq L_\theta(\mathcal H|\theta,I_\theta)\).
        Thus \(|V_{\alpha+1}| \leq^* |I_\theta|\leq^* |I_{\Theta(V_\alpha)}|\), as desired.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->

        We produce the required surjections uniformly in \(\alpha\) by induction. 
        This uniformity handles the limit steps.
        For the successor step, 
        suppose \(|V_{\alpha}| \leq^* |I_\lambda|\), and let us show that \(|V_{\alpha+1}| \leq^* |\Theta(V_\alpha)^\omega|\).

        First, assume \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\).
        Then \(|V_{\alpha+1}| =^* |V_\alpha^\omega| \leq^* |(I_\lambda)^\omega| =^* |\lambda^\omega|\leq^* |\Theta(V_\alpha)^\omega|\). 
        
        Otherwise, let \(\theta = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\), and note that \(\theta \leq \Theta(V_\alpha)\) since \(|(V_\alpha)^\omega| = |V_\alpha|\).
        We use that 
        \(P(\lambda^\omega)\subseteq L_\theta(\mathcal H|\theta,I_\theta)\) by
        \cite[Theorems 10.6 and 10.13]{NairianModels}.
        Since every subset of \(V_\alpha\) can be coded by a subset of \(I_\lambda\),
        it follows that \(V_{\alpha+1}\subseteq L_\theta(\mathcal H|\theta,I_\theta)\).
        Thus \(|V_{\alpha+1}| \leq^* |I_\theta|\leq^* |I_{\Theta(V_\alpha)}|\), as desired.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- prp-8: body_md -->
\label{prp:strongs}
    The set of strong cardinals of \(\mathcal H\) is equal to
    \[S = \{\Theta(V_\alpha) : \alpha \geq \omega\text{ and }\alpha\in \xi\setminus S^\xi_\omega\}\]
    \begin{proof}
        Fix \(\alpha\geq \omega\) and let
        \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\).
        We will show that the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\lambda\) is \(\min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\).
        By \cite[Theorem 19.1]{FailureSquare}, the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\lambda\) is \(\Theta(\lambda^\omega)\).
        If \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\), \cref{lma:V_vs_I} implies
        \(\Theta(V_{\alpha+1}) = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\),
        and clearly \(\Theta(V_{\alpha+1}) = \min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\). Otherwise,
        \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\),
        and clearly \(\Theta(V_{\alpha}) = \min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-8 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Fix \(\alpha\geq \omega\) and let
        \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\).
        We will show that the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\lambda\) is \(\min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\).
        By \cite[Theorem 19.1]{FailureSquare}, the least strong of \(\mathcal H\) above \(\lambda\) is \(\Theta(\lambda^\omega)\).
        If \(\textnormal{cf}(\alpha) = \omega\), \cref{lma:V_vs_I} implies
        \(\Theta(V_{\alpha+1}) = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\),
        and clearly \(\Theta(V_{\alpha+1}) = \min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\). Otherwise,
        \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\),
        and clearly \(\Theta(V_{\alpha}) = \min(S\setminus (\lambda+1))\).
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- thm-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:theta_meas}
    In \(N\), if \(\alpha\geq \omega\) and \(\kappa = \Theta(V_\alpha)\), then 
    \(W_{\kappa}\) is \(\kappa\)-complete, so
    \(\kappa\) is measurable.
    \begin{proof}
        We first consider the case that \(\alpha\) is a successor ordinal. Let \(\lambda = \Theta(V_{\alpha-1})\).
        Since \(\lambda\) has uncountable cofinality, \cref{lma:V_vs_I} implies \(\Theta(V_{\alpha}) = \Theta(\lambda^\omega)\).
        Since \(\Theta(V_{\alpha})\) is a strong cardinal of \(\mathcal H\), it
        is regular by \cite[Theorem 10.20]{NairianModels}.

        Apply \cref{thm:main} in \(N\)
        to \(\eta = \kappa\).
        We check the three criteria from that theorem.
        It is easy to see that for all \(\beta < \kappa\),
        \(\Theta(\beta^\omega)\leq \kappa\).
        By \cref{lma:power_correct},
        for all \(\beta < \kappa\), \(P(\beta) \subseteq \mathcal H(\kappa^\omega)\).
        Finally, \(\textnormal{HOD}\) does not correctly compute any successor cardinals by our HPC assumption \cite{Steel2022Comparison}.

        For the limit case, we use the following claim:
\begin{clm}\label{lma:limit-successor-regular}
    In \(N\), for every limit ordinal \(\alpha\), \[\Theta(V_\alpha) = \left(\sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\right)^+\] and \(\Theta(V_\alpha)\) 
    is regular.
\end{clm}
\begin{proof}
    It is a consequence of ZF that \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = (\sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta))^+\);
    see \cite[Lemma 2.1]{GoldbergCCCP}.
    Work in \(N\). We may assume \(\alpha > \omega\).
    Let \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\).
    Since \(N\) is a minimal Nairian model, 
    it contains no strongly inaccessible cardinals, 
    and so \(\lambda\) is singular.

    Assume towards a contradiction that \(\lambda^+\) is singular, say of cofinality \(\mu < \lambda\).
    Let \(\nu\) be a sufficiently large ordinal.
    By Blue--Larson--Sargsyan
    \cite[Theorem 1.17 and Definitions 6.2 and 8.6]{FailureSquare},
    there is an elementary substructure
    \(X\preceq V_\nu\) such that \(\mu\subseteq X\),
    \(\lambda \in X\),
    and \(|X| \leq^* |V_\beta|\) for some \(\beta < \alpha\). (We use here that one can bound the sizes of levels of the \(\mathsf{N}\)-hierarchy from \cite{FailureSquare} by levels of the  cumulative hierarchy; for example, one can prove by induction that \(|\mathsf{N_\gamma}|\leq^* |V_{\omega+\gamma+2}|\).)

    We now use the argument of \cite[Lemma 3]{UsubaChoicelessLS}.
    Since \(\mu\subseteq X\), \(X\cap\lambda^+\) is cofinal in \(\lambda^+\).
    Consider the partial map \(e:X\times\lambda\to\lambda^+\)
    given by \(e(h,\alpha) = h(\alpha)\) if \(h : \lambda \to \lambda^+\)
    is a function in \(X\).
    For each \(\nu<\lambda\), let
    \(R_\nu=\{e(h,\nu):h\in X\}\),
    so \(\lambda^+ = \bigcup_{\nu < \lambda}R_\nu\) and \(\textnormal{ot}(R_\nu) < \lambda\)
    since \(\Theta(X) \leq \lambda\).
    Let \(\varphi_\nu : \textnormal{ot}(R_\nu)\to R_\nu\) be the increasing enumeration,
    and define a partial function \(\Phi: \lambda\times\lambda\to \lambda^+\)
    by \(\Phi(\nu,\alpha) = \varphi_\nu(\alpha)\).
    Then \(\Phi\) surjects \(\lambda\times \lambda\) onto \(\lambda^+\), a contradiction.
\end{proof}

    We now consider the case that \(\alpha\) is a limit ordinal.
        Let \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_\beta)\). 
        By \cref{lma:limit-successor-regular},
        \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = \lambda^+\) is regular.
        By \cref{thm:monotonicity} and the successor case,
        \(\lambda \leq \kappa_{\lambda^+} \leq \lambda^+\).
        The minimality of \(N\) implies that \(\lambda\) is singular.
        Since \(\kappa_{\lambda^+}\) is regular, \(\kappa_{\lambda^+} = \lambda^+\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-10 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->

        Since \(\Theta(V_{\alpha})\) is a strong cardinal of \(\mathcal H\), it

(We use here that one can bound the sizes of levels of the \(\mathsf{N}\)-hierarchy from \cite{FailureSquare} by levels of the  cumulative hierarchy; for example, one can prove by induction that \(|\mathsf{N_\gamma}|\leq^* |V_{\omega+\gamma+2}|\).)
