<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- cor-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:consistency}
    The theory ``\textnormal{ZF + }there are
    infinitely many consecutive measurable cardinals'' is consistent relative to a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals.
    \begin{proof}
        Blue--Larson--Sargsyan 
        construct minimal Nairian models
        starting with a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals. In these models,
        their calculations \cite[Theorem 1.3(1)--(3)]{FailureSquare},
        together with \cref{lma:V_vs_I}, imply that
        \(\Theta(V_{\omega+n+1}) = \Theta^{+n}\) for all \(n < \omega\).
        By \cref{thm:theta_meas}, this yields
        infinitely many consecutive measurable cardinals.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end cor-4 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Blue--Larson--Sargsyan 
        construct minimal Nairian models
        starting with a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals. In these models,
        their calculations \cite[Theorem 1.3(1)--(3)]{FailureSquare},
        together with \cref{lma:V_vs_I}, imply that
        \(\Theta(V_{\omega+n+1}) = \Theta^{+n}\) for all \(n < \omega\).
        By \cref{thm:theta_meas}, this yields
        infinitely many consecutive measurable cardinals.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lma-7: body_md -->
\label{lma:small-completely-generated-algebra}
    Suppose \(\kappa\) is weakly compact and \(\mathbb B\) is a
    \(\kappa\)-cc complete Boolean algebra completely generated by
    fewer than \(\kappa\) elements. Then \(|\mathbb B|<\kappa\).\qed
<!-- end lma-7 -->
<!-- thm-11: body_md -->
\label{thm:woodin_vopenka}
    Suppose \(X\) is a transitive set and \(M\) is an inner model of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\) such that \(M = \textnormal{HOD}_{M\cup \{X\}}\).
    Let \(g \subseteq \textnormal{Col}(\omega,X)\) be \(V\)-generic and let \(\kappa = \Theta(X^{<\omega})\). Then
    \(g\) belongs to a \(\kappa\)-cc forcing extension of \(M\).
<!-- end thm-11 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
We verify that
    \(M\) has the uniform \(\kappa\)-cover property in 
    \(M_g = \textnormal{HOD}^{V[g]}_{M\cup \{g\}}\) and then cite Bukovsky's criterion
    \cite[Proposition 4.37]{GoldbergUniqueness}.
    Suppose \(I\in M\) and \(f : I \to \textnormal{Ord}\) is a function in \(M_g\). Fix a formula \(\varphi\) and a parameter \(a\in M\) such that \[f(i) = \alpha\iff V[g]\vDash \varphi(i,\alpha,g,a)\]
    For each \(i\in I\), let 
    \[F(i) = \{\alpha\in \textnormal{Ord} : \exists p\in \textnormal{Col}(\omega,X)\, \left(p\Vdash \varphi(i,\alpha,g,a)\right)\}\]
    Then \(F\in M\), and for all \(i \in I\), \(f(i)\in F(i)\) and \(|F(i)| \leq^* |X^{<\omega}|\), so \(\textnormal{ot}(F(i)) < \kappa\).
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- prp-9: body_md -->
\label{prp:measurable-preserves-local-regularity}
    Suppose \(\kappa\) is measurable and \(X\) is a transitive
    set with \(\Theta(X)\leq \kappa\). Then, for
    any inner model \(M\) of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\), the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \(\kappa\) is strongly inaccessible in
        \(M(X)\).
        \item For any normal measure \(W\) on \(\kappa\),
        \(W\)-almost every \(\rho<\kappa\) remains regular in
        \(M(X)\).
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end prp-9 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Fix a measure \(W\) witnessing that \(\kappa\) is measurable.
    Let \(M'\) be the class of sets hereditarily
    ordinal definable from parameters in \(M\cup \{X,W\}\). Then \(M' = \textnormal{HOD}_{M'\cup \{X\}}\). Since \(M\subseteq M'\), it suffices to prove (1) and (2) for \(M'(X)\).

    Applying \cref{thm:woodin_vopenka},
    let \(\mathbb A\) be a \(\kappa\)-cc complete Boolean  
    algebra and \(G\subseteq \mathbb A\) be an \(M'\)-generic filter such that \(M'[G]\) contains a \(V\)-generic collapse \(g : \omega\to X\).
    Let \(\dot x\) be an \(\mathbb A\)-name for
    a real in \(M'[G]\) coding
    \(X\); for example, \(\dot x\) could code the pull-back
    of the membership relation \(\in\restriction X\) under \(\dot g\) to a binary relation on \(\omega\).
    Let \(\mathbb B\) be the complete subalgebra of 
    \(\mathbb A\) generated by the Boolean values of 
    \(\dot x(n) = m\) for \(n,m < \omega\).
    Then in \(M'\), \(\mathbb B\) is a countably generated \(\kappa\)-cc Boolean algebra and
    \(M'(X)\) is contained in a \(\mathbb B\)-generic extension of \(M'\).

    Since \(\kappa\) is measurable in \(M'\), \cref{lma:small-completely-generated-algebra} implies \(|\mathbb B| < \kappa\).
    Therefore \(\kappa\) remains strongly inaccessible in any \(\mathbb B\)-generic extension of \(M'\), and so \(\kappa\) is strongly inaccessible in 
    \(M'(X)\), which proves (1).
    For (2), note that if \(W\) is normal, then \(W\) concentrates on regular cardinals of \(M'\), and forcing with \(\mathbb B\) preserves all sufficiently large \(M'\)-regular cardinals below \(\kappa\).
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- cor-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:no-long-rank-sequences}
    In \(N\), if \(\omega\leq \alpha < \xi\), there is no
    \(\Theta(V_\alpha)\)-sequence of distinct subsets of
    \(V_\alpha\).
<!-- end cor-5 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Let \(\kappa=\Theta(V_\alpha)\), which is measurable by
    \cref{thm:theta_meas}.

    First suppose that \(\alpha\) is a successor ordinal.
    Assume towards
    a contradiction that 
    \(\vec A=\langle A_\nu:\nu<\kappa\rangle\)
    is a sequence of distinct subsets of \(V_\alpha\).
    By \cref{lma:V_vs_I}, for some \(\eta<\kappa\),
    there is a surjection
    \(f:\eta^\omega\to V_\alpha\). (We can take
    \(\eta = \Theta(V_{\alpha-1})\).)
    Let
    \(B_\nu=f^{-1}[A_\nu]\) for \(\nu<\kappa\).
    Choose \(s\in I_\xi\) such that \(f\) and
    \(\vec A\) are \(\textnormal{OD}_s\). By
    \cite[Theorem 12.11]{FailureSquare}, the relation
    \[
        \{(\nu,t)\in\kappa\times\eta^\omega:t\in B_\nu\},
    \]
    has an \(\infty\)-Borel code in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\). Hence
    \(\langle B_\nu:\nu<\kappa\rangle\) belongs to
    \(\textnormal{HOD}_s(\eta^\omega)\).
    By \cref{prp:measurable-preserves-local-regularity}(1),
    \(\kappa\) is strongly inaccessible in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s(\eta^\omega)\),
    contradicting that
    \(\kappa\) injects into \(P(\eta^\omega)\)
    via the map \(f(\nu) = B_\nu\).

    Now suppose that \(\alpha\) is a limit ordinal, and let \(U\) be a nonprincipal
    \(\kappa\)-complete ultrafilter on \(\kappa\). 
    The successor case shows that if
    \(\beta<\alpha\), there is no
    \(\kappa\)-sequence of distinct subsets of \(V_\beta\).
    By \cite[Lemma 3.5]{Goldberg2024MeasurableChoiceless},
    \(U\) is therefore closed under
    \(V_\beta\)-indexed intersections. Since \(\alpha<\kappa\) and \(U\) is \(\kappa\)-complete, this implies that \(U\) is
    closed under \(V_\alpha\)-indexed intersections as well.
    A standard argument now
    shows that there is no \(\kappa\)-sequence of distinct subsets of \(V_\alpha\); see the proof of \cite[Lemma 10.4]{Jech2003SetTheory}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- thm-12: body_md -->
\label{thm:dichotomy}
    Exactly one of the following holds:
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \label{item:eventual_complete} \(N\) satisfies
        that for all \(\kappa\), for all sufficiently large regular \(\delta\),
        \(W_\delta\) is \(\kappa\)-complete.
        \item \label{item:supercompact} \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\) satisfies that there is a supercompact cardinal.
    \end{enumerate}
    \begin{proof}
        Work in \(N^+\).
        Assume \ref{item:eventual_complete} fails. By \cref{thm:monotonicity},
        there is some cardinal \(\kappa_*\) such that for all sufficiently large
        regular \(\delta < \xi\), \(\kappa_\delta = \kappa_*\).
        
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} and \cref{prp:kappa_cov}, for all regular \(\delta \in [\kappa_*,\xi)\), 
        there is some \(s\in I_\xi\) such that
        \(\kappa_*\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover 
        and approximation properties at \(\delta\).
        Let \(\gamma = \kappa_*^+\). 
        By the Hamkins uniqueness theorem \cite[Theorem 4.7]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD},
        if \(M_0\) and \(M_1\) are inner models of ZFC with the
        \(\kappa_*\)-approximation and cover properties at \(\delta\)
        such that \(M_0\cap H(\gamma) = M_1\cap H(\gamma)\),
        then \(M_0 \cap P(\delta) = M_1\cap P(\delta)\).
        By a pigeonhole argument, using that \(\xi\) is inaccessible,
        we can find a single transitive model \(M\subseteq V_\xi\)
        with the \(\kappa_*\)-cover and approximation properties
        such that for arbitrarily large regular \(\delta\),
        there is some \(s\in I_\xi\)
        such that \(P(\delta)\cap M = P(\delta)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and \(\kappa_*\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).

        It follows easily that \(\kappa_*\) is supercompact in \(M\)
        and \(M\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\). Moreover
        \(M\) is ordinal definable from \(x = M\cap H(\gamma)\).
        Thus \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x\). 
        Note also that \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover and approximation properties in \(M\) 
        since \(M\vDash \textnormal{UA}\) and \(\textnormal{UA}\) implies that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) is a weak extender
        model at each supercompact cardinal \cite[Theorem 6.2.15]{UABook}.

        We now leave \(N^+\) and work in the ambient determinacy model. 
        We have that \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x^{N^+}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x\).
        By Vop\v{e}nka's theorem, \(\textnormal{HOD}_x\) is contained in a \(\rho\)-cc
        generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\) for some \(\rho < \xi\). 
        (A coarse upper bound is \(\rho = (\Theta(V_{\gamma+1}))^N\).)
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover property below \(\xi\) 
        and is contained in a \(\rho\)-cc generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\), it follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the
        \((\kappa_*,\rho)\)-cover property below \(\xi\). This covering property
        implies that for all regular \(\delta\in (\rho,\xi)\), \(\textnormal{HOD}\) satisfies
        \(\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta}\leq \rho\). Applying \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov},
        it follows immediately that there is a supercompact cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\).

        Finally, we sketch the proof that \ref{item:eventual_complete} and \ref{item:supercompact}
        are mutually exclusive. Assume \ref{item:supercompact} holds.
        We will show that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals in \(N^+\).
        Granting this, let us show that \ref{item:eventual_complete} fails. 
        Let \(\kappa_0\) be the least supercompact of \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\). 
        By \cite[Lemma 7.4.49]{UABook} if \(\kappa_0\leq \delta < \xi\) and \(\delta\) is a regular cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}\),
        then \(\mathscr K_\delta^0 = (\mathscr K_\delta)^\textnormal{HOD}\) has completeness
        \(\kappa_0\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        If \(\delta > \kappa_0\) is regular in \(N\), then by \cref{prp:W_to_K}
        \(\kappa_\delta\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta^0} = \kappa_0\), which rules out \ref{item:eventual_complete}.
        
        We now show that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals in \(N^+\).
        First, note that \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} shows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa_0)\)-cover
        property below \(\xi\). 
        Thus the same is true for \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for any \(s\in I_\xi\),
        and it follows from \cref{lma:ketonen} that 
        there is a supercompact cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\xi\).
        Let us denote by \(\kappa_s\) the least supercompact cardinal of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\xi\). 
        Thus \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa_s)\)-cover property below \(\xi\).

        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\) with \(\kappa_s\) as small as possible.
        We claim that \(\kappa_s\) is regular in \(N\):
        otherwise, take \(t\in I_\xi\) such that \(s\in \textnormal{HOD}_t\) and
        \(\kappa_s\) is singular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\). Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_t\), 
        \(\kappa_t\leq \kappa_s\), but since \(\kappa_s\) is singular
        in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\), \(\kappa_t < \kappa_s\), contrary to the minimality of \(\kappa_s\).
        The proof of \cite[Theorem 2.8]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD} 
        now shows that if \(\kappa_s \leq \delta < \xi\) and \(\delta\) is regular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\),
        then in \(N\), \(\textnormal{cf}(\delta) = |\delta|\). 
        Since \(\xi\) is a limit cardinal in \(N^+\),
        it follows that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-12 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Work in \(N^+\).
        Assume \ref{item:eventual_complete} fails. By \cref{thm:monotonicity},
        there is some cardinal \(\kappa_*\) such that for all sufficiently large
        regular \(\delta < \xi\), \(\kappa_\delta = \kappa_*\).
        
        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} and \cref{prp:kappa_cov}, for all regular \(\delta \in [\kappa_*,\xi)\), 
        there is some \(s\in I_\xi\) such that
        \(\kappa_*\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover 
        and approximation properties at \(\delta\).
        Let \(\gamma = \kappa_*^+\). 
        By the Hamkins uniqueness theorem \cite[Theorem 4.7]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD},
        if \(M_0\) and \(M_1\) are inner models of ZFC with the
        \(\kappa_*\)-approximation and cover properties at \(\delta\)
        such that \(M_0\cap H(\gamma) = M_1\cap H(\gamma)\),
        then \(M_0 \cap P(\delta) = M_1\cap P(\delta)\).
        By a pigeonhole argument, using that \(\xi\) is inaccessible,
        we can find a single transitive model \(M\subseteq V_\xi\)
        with the \(\kappa_*\)-cover and approximation properties
        such that for arbitrarily large regular \(\delta\),
        there is some \(s\in I_\xi\)
        such that \(P(\delta)\cap M = P(\delta)\cap \textnormal{HOD}_s\)
        and \(\kappa_*\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\).

        It follows easily that \(\kappa_*\) is supercompact in \(M\)
        and \(M\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\). Moreover
        \(M\) is ordinal definable from \(x = M\cap H(\gamma)\).
        Thus \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x\). 
        Note also that \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover and approximation properties in \(M\) 
        since \(M\vDash \textnormal{UA}\) and \(\textnormal{UA}\) implies that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) is a weak extender
        model at each supercompact cardinal \cite[Theorem 6.2.15]{UABook}.

        We now leave \(N^+\) and work in the ambient determinacy model. 
        We have that \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x^{N^+}\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_x\).
        By Vop\v{e}nka's theorem, \(\textnormal{HOD}_x\) is contained in a \(\rho\)-cc
        generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\) for some \(\rho < \xi\). 
        (A coarse upper bound is \(\rho = (\Theta(V_{\gamma+1}))^N\).)
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}^M\) has the \(\kappa_*\)-cover property below \(\xi\) 
        and is contained in a \(\rho\)-cc generic extension of \(\textnormal{HOD}\), it follows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the
        \((\kappa_*,\rho)\)-cover property below \(\xi\). This covering property
        implies that for all regular \(\delta\in (\rho,\xi)\), \(\textnormal{HOD}\) satisfies
        \(\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta}\leq \rho\). Applying \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov},
        it follows immediately that there is a supercompact cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\).

        Finally, we sketch the proof that \ref{item:eventual_complete} and \ref{item:supercompact}
        are mutually exclusive. Assume \ref{item:supercompact} holds.
        We will show that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals in \(N^+\).
        Granting this, let us show that \ref{item:eventual_complete} fails. 
        Let \(\kappa_0\) be the least supercompact of \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\). 
        By \cite[Lemma 7.4.49]{UABook} if \(\kappa_0\leq \delta < \xi\) and \(\delta\) is a regular cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}\),
        then \(\mathscr K_\delta^0 = (\mathscr K_\delta)^\textnormal{HOD}\) has completeness
        \(\kappa_0\) in \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        If \(\delta > \kappa_0\) is regular in \(N\), then by \cref{prp:W_to_K}
        \(\kappa_\delta\leq \kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta^0} = \kappa_0\), which rules out \ref{item:eventual_complete}.
        
        We now show that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals in \(N^+\).
        First, note that \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov} shows that \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa_0)\)-cover
        property below \(\xi\). 
        Thus the same is true for \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) for any \(s\in I_\xi\),
        and it follows from \cref{lma:ketonen} that 
        there is a supercompact cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\xi\).
        Let us denote by \(\kappa_s\) the least supercompact cardinal of \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\cap V_\xi\). 
        Thus \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa_s)\)-cover property below \(\xi\).

        Fix \(s\in I_\xi\) with \(\kappa_s\) as small as possible.
        We claim that \(\kappa_s\) is regular in \(N\):
        otherwise, take \(t\in I_\xi\) such that \(s\in \textnormal{HOD}_t\) and
        \(\kappa_s\) is singular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\). Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\subseteq \textnormal{HOD}_t\), 
        \(\kappa_t\leq \kappa_s\), but since \(\kappa_s\) is singular
        in \(\textnormal{HOD}_t\), \(\kappa_t < \kappa_s\), contrary to the minimality of \(\kappa_s\).
        The proof of \cite[Theorem 2.8]{Goldberg2024StronglyCompactOD} 
        now shows that if \(\kappa_s \leq \delta < \xi\) and \(\delta\) is regular in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\),
        then in \(N\), \(\textnormal{cf}(\delta) = |\delta|\). 
        Since \(\xi\) is a limit cardinal in \(N^+\),
        it follows that \(\xi\) is a limit of regular cardinals.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- prp-10: body_md -->
\label{prp:hpc_no_sc}
    Assuming \(\textnormal{AD}_\mathbb R + \textnormal{HPC} + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\), \cref{thm:dichotomy} \ref{item:eventual_complete} holds.
    \begin{proof}
        Under these assumptions, 
        Steel's analysis of \(\textnormal{HOD}\) as an lbr hod pair \cite{Steel2022Comparison} shows that in \(\textnormal{HOD}\),
        there is no cardinal \(\kappa\) that is \(\kappa^+\)-supercompact.  
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-10 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Under these assumptions, 
        Steel's analysis of \(\textnormal{HOD}\) as an lbr hod pair \cite{Steel2022Comparison} shows that in \(\textnormal{HOD}\),
        there is no cardinal \(\kappa\) that is \(\kappa^+\)-supercompact.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- prp-11: body_md -->
\label{prp:cardinal_no_sc}
    Assuming \(\xi\) is a cardinal in \(V\), \cref{thm:dichotomy} \ref{item:eventual_complete} holds.
    \begin{proof}
        By a theorem of Woodin \cite[Theorem 7.24]{Woodin2017UltimateL}, 
        assuming \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\), if \(\xi\) is a cardinal,
        then in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), no \(\kappa < \xi\) is \({<}\xi\)-supercompact.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-11 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
By a theorem of Woodin \cite[Theorem 7.24]{Woodin2017UltimateL}, 
        assuming \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\), if \(\xi\) is a cardinal,
        then in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), no \(\kappa < \xi\) is \({<}\xi\)-supercompact.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- conj-1: body_md -->
\label{conj:ssh}
    Assume \(\textnormal{AD}^+ + V = L(P(\mathbb R))\). 
    Suppose \(\gamma < \Theta\) and the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \(\gamma\) is a cardinal in \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        \item No ordinal \(\kappa < \gamma\) is \({<}\gamma\)-supercompact in \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        \item \(\textnormal{cf}(\gamma) = \omega\).
    \end{enumerate}
    Then \(\Theta_0(\gamma^\omega)\) is the least Woodin cardinal of \(\textnormal{HOD}\) above \(\gamma\).
<!-- end conj-1 -->
<!-- prp-12: body_md -->
\label{prp:not_vacuous}
    Assume \cref{conj:ssh}. If \(\xi\) is inaccessible in \(\textnormal{HOD}\)
    and \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\) satisfies that there is a supercompact cardinal,
    then \(\xi\) is inaccessible in \(N^+\).
    \begin{proof}
        Let \(\kappa\) be the least supercompact cardinal of \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\).
        We begin by computing \(\Theta_0(\kappa^\omega)\).
        Note that \(\kappa\) is a limit of cardinals \(\gamma\) satisfying
        the hypotheses of \cref{conj:ssh}. 
        Since \(\kappa\) is a limit of Woodins in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), 
        this implies that \(\Theta_0(\gamma^\omega) < \kappa\) for all \(\gamma < \kappa\). 
        Moreover by the \(\textnormal{HOD}\)-ultrafilter hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), \(\textnormal{cf}(\kappa)\geq \omega_1\).
        Therefore \(\Theta_0(\kappa^\omega) = \kappa^{+\textnormal{HOD}}\).
        
        Let \(\mathbb P\in \textnormal{HOD}\) be the product Vop\v{e}nka forcing adding a sequence of elements of
        \(\kappa^\omega\) to \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        By counting ordinal definable \(\infty\)-Borel codes, 
        one can show \(\textnormal{HOD}\vDash |\mathbb P| \leq 2^{<\theta}\) where \(\theta = \Theta_0(\kappa^\omega)\).
        In particular, \(|\mathbb P| < \xi\). 
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\) is an inner model of a \(\mathbb P\)-generic extension of
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\), it follows that \(\xi\) remains inaccessible in \(\textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\).

        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property
        below \(\xi\). Therefore \(N^+ = \textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\),
        and so \(\xi\) remains inaccessible in \(N^+\), proving the proposition.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end prp-12 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Let \(\kappa\) be the least supercompact cardinal of \(\textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\).
        We begin by computing \(\Theta_0(\kappa^\omega)\).
        Note that \(\kappa\) is a limit of cardinals \(\gamma\) satisfying
        the hypotheses of \cref{conj:ssh}. 
        Since \(\kappa\) is a limit of Woodins in \(\textnormal{HOD}\), 
        this implies that \(\Theta_0(\gamma^\omega) < \kappa\) for all \(\gamma < \kappa\). 
        Moreover by the \(\textnormal{HOD}\)-ultrafilter hypothesis (\cref{thm:hod_uf}), \(\textnormal{cf}(\kappa)\geq \omega_1\).
        Therefore \(\Theta_0(\kappa^\omega) = \kappa^{+\textnormal{HOD}}\).
        
        Let \(\mathbb P\in \textnormal{HOD}\) be the product Vop\v{e}nka forcing adding a sequence of elements of
        \(\kappa^\omega\) to \(\textnormal{HOD}\).
        By counting ordinal definable \(\infty\)-Borel codes, 
        one can show \(\textnormal{HOD}\vDash |\mathbb P| \leq 2^{<\theta}\) where \(\theta = \Theta_0(\kappa^\omega)\).
        In particular, \(|\mathbb P| < \xi\). 
        Since \(\textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\) is an inner model of a \(\mathbb P\)-generic extension of
        \(\textnormal{HOD}\), it follows that \(\xi\) remains inaccessible in \(\textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\).

        By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, \(\textnormal{HOD}\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property
        below \(\xi\). Therefore \(N^+ = \textnormal{HOD}_{\kappa^\omega}\),
        and so \(\xi\) remains inaccessible in \(N^+\), proving the proposition.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- thm-13: body_md -->
\label{thm:abn}
    Suppose that there is a class function that assigns
    to each regular cardinal \(\delta\) a uniform measure \(U_\delta\in \textnormal{meas}(\delta)\) so that
    the function \(\delta \mapsto \kappa_{U_\delta}\) is monotone and unbounded. 
    Then there is a class symmetric extension
    satisfying \(\textnormal{ZF}\) in which every uncountable cardinal is singular.
    \begin{proof}
        We may assume there is a proper class of regular cardinals.
        Let \[C = \{\gamma \in \textnormal{Ord} : |\{\kappa_{U_\delta}: \delta\in \textnormal{Reg}\cap \gamma\}| = \gamma\}\]
        and let \(\langle \kappa_i : i \in \textnormal{Ord}\rangle\) enumerate the Beth fixed-points in \(C\) in increasing order.
        (An ordinal \(\kappa\) is a \textit{Beth fixed-point}
        if for all \(a\in V_\kappa\), \(\Theta(a) < \kappa\).)
        Let \(\vec U = \langle U_\delta: \delta\in \textnormal{Reg}\rangle\). 
        Let \(\mathbb P = \mathbb P_\text{fin}(\vec U)\) be Adkisson--Ben-Neria's class forcing 
        from \cite[Definition 56]{AdkissonBenNeria2025}.
        The proofs of \cite[Lemma 57 and Proposition 64]{AdkissonBenNeria2025},
        showing that Adkisson--Ben-Neria's symmetric submodel of \(V^\mathbb P\) 
        satisfies \(\textnormal{ZF}\) plus all uncountable cardinals are singular, 
        ostensibly require the existence of a rank Berkeley cardinal.
        In fact, their proofs go through in our context, with 
        the sequence of almost supercompact cardinals replaced by 
        the sequence \(\langle \kappa_i : i \in \textnormal{Ord}\rangle\) defined above. 
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-13 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
We may assume there is a proper class of regular cardinals.
        Let \[C = \{\gamma \in \textnormal{Ord} : |\{\kappa_{U_\delta}: \delta\in \textnormal{Reg}\cap \gamma\}| = \gamma\}\]
        and let \(\langle \kappa_i : i \in \textnormal{Ord}\rangle\) enumerate the Beth fixed-points in \(C\) in increasing order.
        (An ordinal \(\kappa\) is a \textit{Beth fixed-point}
        if for all \(a\in V_\kappa\), \(\Theta(a) < \kappa\).)
        Let \(\vec U = \langle U_\delta: \delta\in \textnormal{Reg}\rangle\). 
        Let \(\mathbb P = \mathbb P_\text{fin}(\vec U)\) be Adkisson--Ben-Neria's class forcing 
        from \cite[Definition 56]{AdkissonBenNeria2025}.
        The proofs of \cite[Lemma 57 and Proposition 64]{AdkissonBenNeria2025},
        showing that Adkisson--Ben-Neria's symmetric submodel of \(V^\mathbb P\) 
        satisfies \(\textnormal{ZF}\) plus all uncountable cardinals are singular, 
        ostensibly require the existence of a rank Berkeley cardinal.
        In fact, their proofs go through in our context, with 
        the sequence of almost supercompact cardinals replaced by 
        the sequence \(\langle \kappa_i : i \in \textnormal{Ord}\rangle\) defined above.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- cor-6: body_md -->
Assuming \(\textnormal{HPC}\) or that \(\xi\) is a \(V\)-cardinal, 
    \(N\) has a symmetric extension
    satisfying \(\textnormal{ZF}\) in which every uncountable cardinal is singular.\qed
<!-- end cor-6 -->
<!-- cor-7: body_md -->
\label{cor:gitik}
    The theory ``\textnormal{ZF + }every uncountable cardinal is singular'' 
    is consistent relative to a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals.\qed
<!-- end cor-7 -->
<!-- thm-14: body_md -->
\label{thm:derived2}
    In the context of \cref{thm:derived}, for any
    \(\gamma<\Theta\), the model \(M\)
    and the embedding \(j\) can be chosen so that \(j\restriction\gamma\in M\).
<!-- end thm-14 -->
<!-- thm-15: body_md -->
\label{thm:unique}
    In \(N\), 
    for all \(X\), there is a unique normal fine measure on \(P_{\omega_1}(X)\).
<!-- end thm-15 -->
<!-- thm-16: body_md -->
In the context of \cref{thm:spector}, if \(U\) is a normal fine measure on \(P_{\omega_1}(X)\), then \(k([\textnormal{id}]_U) = j[X]\)
    and \(M\vDash |j[X]| \leq \aleph_0\).
<!-- end thm-16 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Suppose \(H\in \textnormal{HOD}\) is a countable transitive model 
        of \(\textnormal{ZF}\) admitting an elementary embedding \(\rho : H \to \mathscr H\)
        with \(\xi\in \textnormal{ran}(\rho)\), and let \(\bar \xi = \rho^{-1}(\xi)\). Applying
        \(\textnormal{AD}_\mathbb R\), let \(\gamma \in (\xi,\Theta)\) be least such that
        every \(s\in I_\xi\) is \({<}\gamma\)-generic over \(\textnormal{HOD}\),
        and let \(\bar \gamma = \rho^{-1}(\gamma)\).

        We will use the construction from \cref{thm:ua}.
        Let \(\tilde{M}\) denote the model \(M\) from 
        \cref{thm:derived2}, and let
        \(M = \rho^{-1}[\tilde{M}]\).
        Let \(D\) be a derived model of \(M\) at the 
        appropriate limit of Woodin cardinals and let
        \(i : H \to \mathscr H^D\) be the embedding
        corresponding to the one from \cref{thm:derived2}.
        We may assume that \(i\restriction \bar \gamma\in M\cap D\).
        In particular, \(M\cap P(\bar \gamma) = H\cap P(\bar \gamma)\).

        Suppose \(\bar{\mathcal N}\in \textnormal{HOD}\) is an outer model of
        \(H\) such that \(\rho\) is partial elementary from
        \((\bar{\mathcal N},H)\) to \((N^+,\mathscr H)\).
        Let \(\bar I = I_{\bar \xi}\cap \bar{\mathcal N}\).
        By our choice of \(\bar \gamma\), every
        \(s\in \bar I\) is \({<}\bar\gamma\)-generic over
        \(H\), and since \(M\cap P(\bar \gamma) = H\cap P(\bar \gamma)\),
        it follows that the same is true for \(M\).

        Let \(\mathbb R^* = \mathbb R\cap \tilde{M}\), and
        as in \cref{thm:ua},
        let \(\pi : M \to M^*\) be a Neeman-style \(\mathbb R^*\)-genericity
        iteration with critical point above \(\bar \gamma\). 
        Let \(D^*\) be the derived model of \(M^*\) computed in
        \(M^*(\mathbb R^*)\), and recall as in 
        \cref{thm:ua} that \(P(\mathbb R^*)\cap D^* \unlhd \textnormal{Hom}^{\tilde{M}}\). 
        
        Note that \(k = \pi \circ i\) is an elementary embedding
        from \(H\) to \(H^* = \mathscr H^{D^*}\).
        Moreover, since
        \(i\restriction \bar \xi\in M\cap D\) and \(\textnormal{crit}(\pi) > \bar \xi\),
        we have \(k\restriction \bar\xi = 
        \pi(i\restriction \bar\xi) \in D^*\).
        We claim that \(k\) lifts to an elementary embedding
        \[\hat{k} : (\bar{\mathcal N},H)\to (\mathcal N^*,H^*)\]
        where \(\mathcal N^* = (N_{k(\bar \xi)}^+)^{D^*}\).
        Namely, if \(a\in \bar{\mathcal N}\) is definable in 
        \((\bar{\mathcal N},H)\) from \(s\in \bar I\), set \(\hat{k}(a)\) 
        equal to the element of \(\mathcal N^*\) defined 
        in \((\mathcal N^*,H^*)\) from \(k[s]\) in the same way.
        Note here that \(k[s]\in D^*\) since \(k\restriction \bar\xi\in D^*\).
        The fact that \(\hat{k}\) is well-defined and elementary follows from
        the fact that \(\pi\circ i\) extends, for each \(s\in \bar I\),
        to an elementary embedding from \(H_s\) to \(\mathscr H^{D^*}_{k[s]}\),
        using \cref{thm:derived2} and the fact that \(\textnormal{crit}(\pi) > \bar\gamma\).

        Since it will be relevant in \cref{thm:ua_N},
        we note that the embedding \(\hat k\) is close to \(\bar{\mathcal N}\). Fix ordinals \(\delta\) and \(\alpha < k(\delta)\), and let
        \(U\) be the \(\bar{\mathcal N}\)-measure on \(\delta\) derived from \(\hat{k}\) using \(\alpha\).
        Let 
        \(\tilde{U}\) be the \(M\)-ultrafilter on \(i(\delta)\) derived from \(\pi\) using \(\alpha\). 
        Since \(\pi\) is close to \(M\), \(\tilde{U}\in M\). Moreover, since \(\textnormal{crit}(\pi) > \bar\gamma\), \(\kappa_{\tilde{U}} > \bar\gamma\),
        and therefore for each \(s\in \bar I\), Levy--Solovay implies
        \(\tilde{U}\) generates a measure \(\tilde{U}_s\) 
        in \(M[s] = M[i[s]]\).
        Finally, \[U = \{A\in P(\delta)\cap \bar{\mathcal N} : \exists s\in \bar I\, (A\in H[s]\wedge i_s(A)\in \tilde{U}_s)\}\]
        and this is definable over 
        \((\bar{\mathcal N},H)\) from the parameter 
        \(\tilde{U}\in M\subseteq H\).

        Now fix a finitely generated structure \(H\)
        with an elementary \(\rho : H \to \mathscr H\)
        such that \(\rho(\bar \xi) = \xi\) and \(\rho(\bar \Theta) = \Theta\).
        Construct \(\bar {V}\in \textnormal{HOD}\) by a type omission argument
        so that \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar {V}}\).
        Let \(\bar{\mathcal N} = (N_{\bar \xi}^+)^{\bar V}\).

        Suppose that \(X,\mathcal U\in \bar{\mathcal N}\) and \(\bar{\mathcal N}\)
        satisfies that \(\mathcal U\) is
        a normal measure on \(P_{\omega_1}(X)\). 
        We will show that \(\mathcal U\) is the measure derived from
        the embedding \(\hat{k} : \bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal N^*\)
        defined above using \(\hat{k}[X]\).

        Using Spector's ultrapower construction, form in \(\textnormal{HOD}\)
        an elementary \(j : \bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal P\)
        such that \(\mathcal U\) is the measure
        derived from \(j\) using \(j[X]\) and \(j\restriction (P(\textnormal{Ord})\cap \bar{\mathcal N})\)
        agrees with the ultrapower embedding of \(\mathcal U\).
        Let \(H_{\mathcal P} = j(H)\). 
        By a standard realizability argument, there is an elementary embedding
        \(\rho_{\mathcal P} : H_{\mathcal P}\to \mathscr H\)
        such that \(\rho = \rho_{\mathcal P}\circ j\restriction H\).
        This embedding is automatically partial elementary from \(\mathcal P\) to \(N^+\) since the first-order theory of \(N^+\) with ordinal parameters uniformly reduces to that of \(\mathscr H\).

        Applying the construction above to \(\mathcal P\) instead of \(\bar{\mathcal N}\),
        we obtain \(\hat{k}_{\mathcal P} : \mathcal P \to \mathcal N^*_{\mathcal P}\).
        Using that \(H\) is finitely generated, one 
        can show as in \cref{thm:ua} that the relevant derived models are equal.
        Moreover,
        \(\hat{k}\restriction H = \hat{k}_{\mathcal P}\circ j\restriction H\) since \(H\) is finitely generated
        and these are close embeddings. 
        We can now conclude that \(\mathcal N^*_{\mathcal P} = \mathcal N^*\). 
        Moreover, it follows that
        \(\hat{k}\) and \(\hat{k}_{\mathcal P}\circ j\) agree on \(\bar I\), and hence on \(\bar{\mathcal N}\).
        In particular, \(\hat{k}_{\mathcal P}(j[X]) = \hat{k}_{\mathcal P}\circ j[X] = \hat{k}[X]\).
        Since \(\mathcal U\) is derived from \(j\) using \(j[X]\), we also have that
        \(\mathcal U\) is derived from \(\hat{k}_{\mathcal P}\circ j\) using \(\hat{k}_{\mathcal P}(j[X])\). 
        In other words, \(\mathcal U\) is the measure derived from \(\hat{k}\) using \(\hat{k}[X]\).
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- thm-17: body_md -->
\label{thm:ua_N}
    The Ultrapower Axiom holds in \(N^+\).
    \begin{proof}
        Fix a finitely generated structure \(H\)
        with an elementary \(\rho : H \to \mathscr H\)
        such that \(\rho(\bar \xi) = \xi\) and \(\rho(\bar \Theta) = \Theta\).
        Construct \(\bar {V}\in \textnormal{HOD}\) by \cref{prp:hod-realization}
        so that \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar {V}}\).
        We will prove that \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\bar{\mathcal N} = (N_{\bar \xi}^+)^{\bar V}\).

        Suppose \(j_0 :\bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal P_0\) and 
        \(j_1 : \bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal P_1\) are Spector ultrapowers
        of \(\bar{\mathcal N}\) by \(\bar{\mathcal N}\)-internal measures 
        \(U_0\) and \(U_1\) on ordinals.
        The construction from \cref{thm:unique} produces close embeddings
        \(k_0 : \mathcal P_0\to M\) and \(k_1 : \mathcal P_1\to M\)
        such that \(k_0\circ j_{0} = k_1\circ j_{1}\).
        The arguments from \cite{UAFromDet} show this implies that \(\textnormal{UA}\)
        holds for \(U_0\) and \(U_1\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-17 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Fix a finitely generated structure \(H\)
        with an elementary \(\rho : H \to \mathscr H\)
        such that \(\rho(\bar \xi) = \xi\) and \(\rho(\bar \Theta) = \Theta\).
        Construct \(\bar {V}\in \textnormal{HOD}\) by \cref{prp:hod-realization}
        so that \(H = \mathscr H^{\bar {V}}\).
        We will prove that \(\textnormal{UA}\) holds in \(\bar{\mathcal N} = (N_{\bar \xi}^+)^{\bar V}\).

        Suppose \(j_0 :\bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal P_0\) and 
        \(j_1 : \bar{\mathcal N}\to \mathcal P_1\) are Spector ultrapowers
        of \(\bar{\mathcal N}\) by \(\bar{\mathcal N}\)-internal measures 
        \(U_0\) and \(U_1\) on ordinals.
        The construction from \cref{thm:unique} produces close embeddings
        \(k_0 : \mathcal P_0\to M\) and \(k_1 : \mathcal P_1\to M\)
        such that \(k_0\circ j_{0} = k_1\circ j_{1}\).
        The arguments from \cite{UAFromDet} show this implies that \(\textnormal{UA}\)
        holds for \(U_0\) and \(U_1\).
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- defn-9: body_md -->
We say \(\delta\) is an \textit{NT-cardinal} if the following hold for some infinite \(\alpha\):
    \begin{itemize}
        \item  \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha) = \aleph(V_{\alpha+1})\).
        \item \(V_{\alpha+1}\) is the union of an increasing sequence  \((\Gamma_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\) such that \(|\Gamma_\beta|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\).
        \item If \(M\) is transitive and \(M^{V_\alpha}\subseteq M\), there is \(X\preceq M\) with \(X^{V_\alpha}\subseteq X\) and \(|X|\leq^* |V_{\alpha+1}|\).
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end defn-9 -->
<!-- thm-18: body_md -->
\label{thm:NT-filter-extension}
    Let \(\mathcal H = \textnormal{HOD}\cap V_\xi\) and work in \(N\). Suppose \(\lambda\) is an NT-cardinal or a limit of NT-cardinals such that \(\Theta(P(\rho)) \leq \lambda\) whenever \(\rho^+ < \lambda\).
    Then one of the following holds: 
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item \label{item:filter_extension} Every \(\lambda\)-complete filter on an ordinal
    extends to a \(\lambda\)-complete ultrafilter.
        \item \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\lambda^{++}\).
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-18 -->
<!-- lma-8: body_md -->
\label{lma:nt_inacc}
    If \(\delta\) is an NT-cardinal, then \(\delta\) is regular
    and \(\delta\) is inaccessible in any inner model of \(\textnormal{ZFC}\).
    \begin{proof}
        Fix \(\alpha\) such that \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\).
        By \(\textnormal{DC}\), \(\delta\) has uncountable cofinality.
        To see \(\delta\) is regular, note that combining the final two clauses of the definition of an NT-cardinal, one obtains that 
        for all sufficiently large ordinals \(\theta\), for any \(\gamma < \delta\) and \(a\in V_\theta\), there is some \(X\preceq V_{\theta+1}\) with \(\gamma\cup\{a\}\subseteq X\) and \(|X| \leq^* |V_\alpha|\). An argument similar to \cref{lma:limit-successor-regular} from \cref{thm:theta_meas} then implies \(\delta\) is regular.

        Now to see \(\delta\) is inaccessible in any inner model \(M\) of ZFC, we just need to show \(\delta\) is a strong limit cardinal in \(M\). For any \(\rho < \delta\), \(|\rho|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\), and so \(|P(\rho)|\leq |V_{\alpha+1}|\), which implies \(|P(\rho)\cap M| < \aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \delta\).
        Thus \((2^\rho)^M < |P(\rho)\cap M|^+\leq \delta\). 
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-8 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Fix \(\alpha\) such that \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\).
        By \(\textnormal{DC}\), \(\delta\) has uncountable cofinality.
        To see \(\delta\) is regular, note that combining the final two clauses of the definition of an NT-cardinal, one obtains that 
        for all sufficiently large ordinals \(\theta\), for any \(\gamma < \delta\) and \(a\in V_\theta\), there is some \(X\preceq V_{\theta+1}\) with \(\gamma\cup\{a\}\subseteq X\) and \(|X| \leq^* |V_\alpha|\). An argument similar to \cref{lma:limit-successor-regular} from \cref{thm:theta_meas} then implies \(\delta\) is regular.

        Now to see \(\delta\) is inaccessible in any inner model \(M\) of ZFC, we just need to show \(\delta\) is a strong limit cardinal in \(M\). For any \(\rho < \delta\), \(|\rho|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\), and so \(|P(\rho)|\leq |V_{\alpha+1}|\), which implies \(|P(\rho)\cap M| < \aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \delta\).
        Thus \((2^\rho)^M < |P(\rho)\cap M|^+\leq \delta\).
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lma-9: body_md -->
\label{lma:hod_transfer}
    Suppose \(\delta\) is a regular cardinal
    and \(\kappa_\delta < \delta\).
    \begin{enumerate}[(1)]
        \item\label{item:strong_limit} If there is a strong limit cardinal \(\gamma\) such that \(\kappa_\delta < \gamma \leq \delta\leq \gamma^+\), then \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).
        \item\label{item:NT} If \(\delta\) is an NT-cardinal, then \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).

    \end{enumerate}
    \begin{proof}
        Let \(\kappa = \kappa_\delta\). Applying \cref{cor:ua_completeness}, fix \(s\) such that \(\kappa = (\kappa_{\mathscr K_\delta})^{\mathcal H_s}\). By 
        \cref{prp:kappa_cov}, every \(S\subseteq \delta\)
        belongs to \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) for some \(x\subseteq \kappa\).

        First assume \ref{item:strong_limit}. Then for all \(x\subseteq \kappa\), \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) is a \(\gamma\)-cc generic extension of \(\mathcal H_s\).
        Thus as in \cref{thm:main},
        \(\mathcal H_s\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).

        By the definition of a strong limit cardinal, we have that \(\Theta(P(\kappa^+)) \leq \gamma\). Applying the Hamkins uniqueness theorem as in \cref{thm:main},
        every bounded subset of \(\gamma^{+\omega}\) belongs to
        a \(\gamma\)-cc extension of \(\mathcal H\), and
        therefore \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).

        Instead assume \ref{item:NT}, and fix \(\alpha\) such that
        \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\).
        We claim that in this case, we can pick \(s\) such that
        \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) is a 
        \(\delta\)-cc generic extension of \(\mathcal H_s\) for each \(x\subseteq \kappa\).
        To achieve this, we must choose \(s\) so that in addition
        \(P(\kappa) = \bigcup_{\beta< \delta}S_\beta\)
        with \(|S_\beta|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\).
        Using that each \(S_\beta\) belongs to \(\mathcal O_s\), Bukovsky's criterion \cite{Bukovsky,FriedmanFuchinoSakai} 
        implies \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) is a \(\delta\)-cc
        generic extension of \(\mathcal H_s\).

        Now as in \cref{thm:main}, we obtain that
        \(\mathcal H_s\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\delta\) and \(\kappa\) is \({<}\delta^{+\omega}\)-supercompact in \(\mathcal H_t\) for all \(t\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that \(\mathcal H_s\subseteq \mathcal H_t\). 

        We claim that \(\Theta(P(\delta)) \leq \delta^{++}\).
        Fix a surjection \(f : P(\delta)\to \eta\), and let us show that \(\eta < \delta^{++}\).
        First note that
        \(\Theta(P(\kappa))\leq \delta^+\)
        since \(P(\kappa) = \bigcup_{\beta< \delta}S_\beta\)
        where \(|S_\beta|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\).

        Moreover \(P(\delta) = \bigcup_{a\in P(\kappa)} Y_a\) 
        where \(Y_a = P(\delta)\cap \mathcal H_{s,a}\).
        Note that \(\mathcal H_{s,a}\vDash 2^\delta = \delta^+\): fix \(t\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that
        \(\mathcal H_{s,a}\subseteq \mathcal H_t\); since
        \(\mathcal H_t\) satisfies \(\textnormal{UA}\) plus
        \(\kappa\) is \({<}\delta^{+\omega}\)-supercompact,
        we have \(2^\delta = \delta^+\) in \(\mathcal H_t\) by \cite{UAGCH}; since \(\mathcal H_{s,a}\) is cardinal correct in the interval \([\delta,\delta^{+\omega})\), we obtain \(2^\delta = \delta^+\) in \(\mathcal H_{s,a}\).
        Thus using the canonical well-order of \(\mathcal H_{s,a}\), we can fix bijections \(g_a : \delta^+\to Y_a\).
        Now if \(f : P(\delta)\to \eta\),
        then 
        \[\eta = \bigcup_{a\in P(\kappa)} f[Y_a] = \bigcup_{a\in P(\kappa)} (f\circ g_a)[\delta^+]\]
        It follows that \(|\eta|\leq^* |P(\kappa)\times \delta^+|\), and using \(\Theta(P(\kappa)) \leq \delta^+\), we obtain that \(\eta < \delta^{++}\).

        Applying the Hamkins uniqueness theorem as in \cref{thm:main}, it follows that
        every bounded subset of \(\delta^{+\omega}\) belongs to
        a \(\delta^{++}\)-cc extension of \(\mathcal H\), and
        therefore \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-9 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->

        \cref{prp:kappa_cov}, every \(S\subseteq \delta\)
        belongs to \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) for some \(x\subseteq \kappa\).

        First assume \ref{item:strong_limit}. Then for all \(x\subseteq \kappa\), \(\mathcal H_{s,x}\) is a \(\gamma\)-cc generic extension of \(\mathcal H_s\).
        Thus as in \cref{thm:main},
        \(\mathcal H_s\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).

        every bounded subset of \(\gamma^{+\omega}\) belongs to
        a \(\gamma\)-cc extension of \(\mathcal H\), and
        therefore \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).

        We claim that in this case, we can pick \(s\) such that

        generic extension of \(\mathcal H_s\).

        \(\mathcal H_s\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\delta\) and \(\kappa\) is \({<}\delta^{+\omega}\)-supercompact in \(\mathcal H_t\) for all \(t\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that \(\mathcal H_s\subseteq \mathcal H_t\). 

        We claim that \(\Theta(P(\delta)) \leq \delta^{++}\).
        Fix a surjection \(f : P(\delta)\to \eta\), and let us show that \(\eta < \delta^{++}\).
        First note that
        \(\Theta(P(\kappa))\leq \delta^+\)
        since \(P(\kappa) = \bigcup_{\beta< \delta}S_\beta\)
        where \(|S_\beta|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\).

        Moreover \(P(\delta) = \bigcup_{a\in P(\kappa)} Y_a\) 
        where \(Y_a = P(\delta)\cap \mathcal H_{s,a}\).

        \(\kappa\) is \({<}\delta^{+\omega}\)-supercompact,

<!-- defn-10: body_md -->
If \(U\) is an ultrafilter on \(X\) and \(P\) and \(Q\) are partitions of \(X\),
then \textit{\(P\) refines \(Q\) modulo \(U\)}, denoted \(P\leq_U Q\), 
if there is some \(A\in U\)
such that \(P\restriction A\) refines \(Q\restriction A\).

We say \(U\) is \textit{\(\gamma\)-decomposable} if there is a
partition \(P\) of \(X\) such that \(|P| = \gamma\) and 
whenever \(Q\leq_U P\),
\(|Q| \geq \gamma\). The \textit{decomposability spectrum} \(D(U)\) consists of all \(\gamma\)
such that \(U\) is \(\gamma\)-decomposable.
<!-- end defn-10 -->
<!-- lma-10: body_md -->
\label{lma:limit_spectrum}
    Suppose \(\gamma\) is a strong limit cardinal of countable cofinality or a limit of such cardinals. If there is a measure \(U\) on an ordinal
    with \(\sup(D(U)\cap \gamma) = \gamma\), then \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite successors of \(\gamma\).
    \begin{proof}
        First assume \(\textnormal{cf}(\gamma) = \omega\). 
        Let \(\eta\) be the underlying ordinal of \(U\) and pick 
        \(\gamma_n\in D(U)\) increasing to \(\gamma\) and functions
        \(f_n : \eta\to \gamma_n\) witnessing \(\gamma_n\in D(U)\).
        Fix \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that \((f_n)_{n < \omega}\in \mathcal H_s\).
        Thus in \(\mathcal H_s\), 
        \(\textnormal{cf}(\gamma) = \omega\) and \(\gamma = \sup (D(\bar U)\cap \gamma)\)
        where \(\bar U = U\cap \mathcal H_s\). 
        The proof of \cite[Lemma 5.4]{GoldbergUniqueness} shows that
        in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there is a countably complete uniform ultrafilter
        on \(\delta = (\gamma^+)^{ \mathcal H_s}\). 
        Therefore by \cite[Corollary 7.4.10]{UABook}
        some ordinal \(\kappa < \gamma\) is 
        \(\delta\)-supercompact in \( \mathcal H_s\). 
        The proof of \cite[Lemma 5.4]{GoldbergUniqueness} shows moreover that \(\delta = \gamma^+\): let
        \[X = \{\langle f_n(\xi) : {n < \omega}\rangle : \xi < \eta\}\] and note that \(X\in \mathcal H_s\), \(X\subseteq \gamma^\omega\), and \(|X| \geq \gamma^+\); therefore using Solovay's theorem on the SCH above strongly compact cardinals, \[\delta = (\gamma^+)^{ \mathcal H_s} = (\gamma^\omega)^{\mathcal H_s}\geq \gamma^+\] It follows that \(\delta\) is regular and
        \(\kappa_{\delta} < \delta\). By \cref{lma:hod_transfer},
        \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite
        successors of \(\gamma\).

        We now consider the case where \(\gamma\) has uncountable cofinality. The previous case implies that there is an \(\omega\)-club \(C\) of cardinals \(\bar \gamma < \gamma\)
        such that \((\bar \gamma^+)^{\mathcal H} = \bar\gamma^+\).

        If \(\gamma\) is singular, then a Silver-style argument establishes
        that \((\gamma^{+})^{\mathcal H} = \gamma^+\).
        Namely, let \(\iota = \textnormal{cf}(\gamma)\), and let
        \(j : V \to M\) be the Spector ultrapower via \(W_\iota\).
        Note that \(\sup j[\gamma] = \gamma\) since \(\gamma\) is a strong limit, and hence \(\gamma\in j(C)\).
        Also \((\gamma^{+})^M\geq \gamma^+\): if \(\preceq\) is a well-order of \(\gamma\), then \({\preceq}^* = j({\preceq})\restriction \gamma\) is a well-order of \(\gamma\) that belongs to \(M\) with \(\textnormal{ot}({\preceq}^*)\) at least \(\textnormal{ot}({\preceq})\).
        This implies
        \[(\gamma^+)^{\mathcal H} \geq (\gamma^+)^{j(\mathcal H)} \geq (\gamma^+)^M \geq \gamma^+\]
        Thus \(\gamma^+\) is regular and we can apply \cref{lma:hod_transfer} to \(\delta = \gamma^+\);
        note that \(\kappa_{\gamma^+} < \gamma\) since 
        \(\kappa_{\gamma^+}\) is regular.

        If \(\gamma\) is regular, we can apply \cref{lma:hod_transfer}
        directly to \(\delta = \gamma\) once we show that
        \(\kappa_\gamma < \gamma\).
        Assume towards a contradiction that \(\kappa_{W_\gamma} = \gamma\). 
        Note that for a \(W_\gamma\)-large set of \(\bar \gamma\),
        \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes \(\bar\gamma^+\) and hence \(\mathcal H\) 
        satisfies that some \(\kappa < \bar \gamma\) is
        \(\bar \gamma^+\)-supercompact.
        Since \(W_\gamma\) is normal, 
        there is a fixed \(\kappa < \gamma\) that is \({<}\gamma\)-supercompact in \(\mathcal H\). By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, it follows that \(\mathcal H\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\gamma\),
        and by \cref{lma:ketonen},
        it follows that \((\kappa_{\mathscr K_\gamma})^{\mathcal H}\leq \kappa < \gamma\). By \cref{cor:ua_completeness}, this contradicts that \(\kappa_\gamma = \gamma\). 
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-10 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
First assume \(\textnormal{cf}(\gamma) = \omega\). 
        Let \(\eta\) be the underlying ordinal of \(U\) and pick 
        \(\gamma_n\in D(U)\) increasing to \(\gamma\) and functions
        \(f_n : \eta\to \gamma_n\) witnessing \(\gamma_n\in D(U)\).
        Fix \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that \((f_n)_{n < \omega}\in \mathcal H_s\).
        Thus in \(\mathcal H_s\), 
        \(\textnormal{cf}(\gamma) = \omega\) and \(\gamma = \sup (D(\bar U)\cap \gamma)\)
        where \(\bar U = U\cap \mathcal H_s\). 
        The proof of \cite[Lemma 5.4]{GoldbergUniqueness} shows that
        in \(\textnormal{HOD}_s\), there is a countably complete uniform ultrafilter
        on \(\delta = (\gamma^+)^{ \mathcal H_s}\). 
        Therefore by \cite[Corollary 7.4.10]{UABook}
        some ordinal \(\kappa < \gamma\) is 
        \(\delta\)-supercompact in \( \mathcal H_s\). 
        The proof of \cite[Lemma 5.4]{GoldbergUniqueness} shows moreover that \(\delta = \gamma^+\): let
        \[X = \{\langle f_n(\xi) : {n < \omega}\rangle : \xi < \eta\}\] and note that \(X\in \mathcal H_s\), \(X\subseteq \gamma^\omega\), and \(|X| \geq \gamma^+\); therefore using Solovay's theorem on the SCH above strongly compact cardinals, \[\delta = (\gamma^+)^{ \mathcal H_s} = (\gamma^\omega)^{\mathcal H_s}\geq \gamma^+\] It follows that \(\delta\) is regular and
        \(\kappa_{\delta} < \delta\). By \cref{lma:hod_transfer},
        \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes all finite
        successors of \(\gamma\).

        We now consider the case where \(\gamma\) has uncountable cofinality. The previous case implies that there is an \(\omega\)-club \(C\) of cardinals \(\bar \gamma < \gamma\)
        such that \((\bar \gamma^+)^{\mathcal H} = \bar\gamma^+\).

        If \(\gamma\) is singular, then a Silver-style argument establishes
        that \((\gamma^{+})^{\mathcal H} = \gamma^+\).
        Namely, let \(\iota = \textnormal{cf}(\gamma)\), and let
        \(j : V \to M\) be the Spector ultrapower via \(W_\iota\).
        Note that \(\sup j[\gamma] = \gamma\) since \(\gamma\) is a strong limit, and hence \(\gamma\in j(C)\).
        Also \((\gamma^{+})^M\geq \gamma^+\): if \(\preceq\) is a well-order of \(\gamma\), then \({\preceq}^* = j({\preceq})\restriction \gamma\) is a well-order of \(\gamma\) that belongs to \(M\) with \(\textnormal{ot}({\preceq}^*)\) at least \(\textnormal{ot}({\preceq})\).
        This implies
        \[(\gamma^+)^{\mathcal H} \geq (\gamma^+)^{j(\mathcal H)} \geq (\gamma^+)^M \geq \gamma^+\]
        Thus \(\gamma^+\) is regular and we can apply \cref{lma:hod_transfer} to \(\delta = \gamma^+\);
        note that \(\kappa_{\gamma^+} < \gamma\) since 
        \(\kappa_{\gamma^+}\) is regular.

        If \(\gamma\) is regular, we can apply \cref{lma:hod_transfer}
        directly to \(\delta = \gamma\) once we show that
        \(\kappa_\gamma < \gamma\).
        Assume towards a contradiction that \(\kappa_{W_\gamma} = \gamma\). 
        Note that for a \(W_\gamma\)-large set of \(\bar \gamma\),
        \(\mathcal H\) correctly computes \(\bar\gamma^+\) and hence \(\mathcal H\) 
        satisfies that some \(\kappa < \bar \gamma\) is
        \(\bar \gamma^+\)-supercompact.
        Since \(W_\gamma\) is normal, 
        there is a fixed \(\kappa < \gamma\) that is \({<}\gamma\)-supercompact in \(\mathcal H\). By \cref{prp:gamma_kappa_cov}, it follows that \(\mathcal H\) has the \((\aleph_1,\kappa)\)-cover property at \(\gamma\),
        and by \cref{lma:ketonen},
        it follows that \((\kappa_{\mathscr K_\gamma})^{\mathcal H}\leq \kappa < \gamma\). By \cref{cor:ua_completeness}, this contradicts that \(\kappa_\gamma = \gamma\).
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- defn-11: body_md -->
A \textit{finest partition of \(U\) below \(\lambda\)} is
a \(\leq_U\)-minimum element 
of the set of \({<}\lambda\)-sized partitions of the underlying set of \(U\).
<!-- end defn-11 -->
<!-- lma-11: body_md -->
\label{lma:NT_spectrum}
    Suppose \(\delta\) is an NT-cardinal, \(U\) is a measure on an ordinal, and one of the following holds: 
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \(\sup (D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\) where \(\theta = \aleph(P(\delta))\).
        \item \(\mathcal H\) does not correctly compute all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
    \end{itemize}
    Then \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\delta\).
    \begin{proof}
        Fix \(\alpha\) such that \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\). 
        Fix \(\theta\) sufficiently large and take a \(V_\alpha\)-closed \(X\preceq V_{\theta+1}\) with \(|X| \leq^* |V_{\alpha+1}|\) containing the finitely many relevant parameters. Write 
        \[X = \bigcup_{\beta < \delta} X_\beta\] where \(|X_\beta| \leq^* |V_\alpha|\). Note that each \(X_\beta\in X\) by \(V_\alpha\)-closure. 
        Let \(\eta\) be the underlying ordinal of \(U\) and for \(\beta < \delta\), define a partition \(P_\beta\) of \(\eta\) by putting \(\xi_0,\xi_1 < \eta\) into the same cell if and only if \(D(X_\beta,\xi_0) = D(X_\beta,\xi_1)\) where \[D(X_\beta,\xi) = \{A\in P(\eta)\cap X_\beta: \xi\in A\}\] 
        Then \(P_\beta\in X\) since \(X_\beta\in X\).
        Moreover, we claim that \(|P_\beta| < \delta\). 
        First, note that \(P_\beta\) injects into \(P(X_\beta)\):
        map a cell \(B\in P_\beta\) to the common value
        of \(D(X_\beta,\xi)\) for all \(\xi\in B\).
        Since \(P_\beta\) is well-orderable, 
        \[|P_\beta| < \aleph(P(X_\beta)) \leq \aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \delta\]

        To show \(U\) has a finest partition, it suffices to show that this sequence stabilizes modulo \(U\), or in other words that there is some \(\beta < \delta\) such that for all \(\beta'\geq \beta\), \(P_\beta \equiv P_{\beta'}\). 
        Then since \(P_\beta\in X\), \(P_\beta\) is a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\delta\).

        Assume first that \(\sup(D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\) where \(\theta = \aleph(P(\delta))\). Let \(Q\) be the least common refinement of \((P_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\). Note that \(|Q|\leq |P(\delta)|\). Since \(Q\) is well-orderable, 
        \(|Q| < \aleph(P(\delta))\). Since \(\sup(D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\), there is some \(A\in U\) such that \(|Q\restriction A| < \delta\).
        Thus there is then some \(\beta < \delta\) such that for all \(\beta'\geq \beta\), \(P_\beta\restriction A = P_{\beta'} \restriction A\). 
        Since \(P_\beta\in X\), \(P_\beta\) is a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\delta\).

        We finish by proving \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\delta\) in the case that \(\mathcal H\) does not correctly compute all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
        Take \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that
        \(\langle P_\beta : \beta < \delta\rangle\in \mathcal H_s\).
        Let \(\bar U = U\cap \mathcal H_s\) and let \(\mu = \sup (D(\bar U)\cap \delta)\). If \(\mu < \delta\), then since \(\delta\) is inaccessible in \(\mathcal H_s\), the sequence \((P_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\) stabilizes modulo \(U\): there cannot be a strictly Rudin--Keisler increasing \(\delta\)-sequence of measures on \(\mu\).

        So assume towards a contradiction that
        \(\mu = \delta\).
        Then some \(\kappa < \delta\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact 
        in \(\mathcal H_s\). 
        It follows from \cref{cor:ua_completeness} that \(\kappa_\delta < \delta\),
        and so by \cref{lma:hod_transfer}, \(\mathcal H\) correctly
        computes all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
    \end{proof}
<!-- end lma-11 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Fix \(\alpha\) such that \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\). 
        Fix \(\theta\) sufficiently large and take a \(V_\alpha\)-closed \(X\preceq V_{\theta+1}\) with \(|X| \leq^* |V_{\alpha+1}|\) containing the finitely many relevant parameters. Write 
        \[X = \bigcup_{\beta < \delta} X_\beta\] where \(|X_\beta| \leq^* |V_\alpha|\). Note that each \(X_\beta\in X\) by \(V_\alpha\)-closure. 
        Let \(\eta\) be the underlying ordinal of \(U\) and for \(\beta < \delta\), define a partition \(P_\beta\) of \(\eta\) by putting \(\xi_0,\xi_1 < \eta\) into the same cell if and only if \(D(X_\beta,\xi_0) = D(X_\beta,\xi_1)\) where \[D(X_\beta,\xi) = \{A\in P(\eta)\cap X_\beta: \xi\in A\}\] 
        Then \(P_\beta\in X\) since \(X_\beta\in X\).
        Moreover, we claim that \(|P_\beta| < \delta\). 
        First, note that \(P_\beta\) injects into \(P(X_\beta)\):
        map a cell \(B\in P_\beta\) to the common value
        of \(D(X_\beta,\xi)\) for all \(\xi\in B\).
        Since \(P_\beta\) is well-orderable, 
        \[|P_\beta| < \aleph(P(X_\beta)) \leq \aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \delta\]

        To show \(U\) has a finest partition, it suffices to show that this sequence stabilizes modulo \(U\), or in other words that there is some \(\beta < \delta\) such that for all \(\beta'\geq \beta\), \(P_\beta \equiv P_{\beta'}\). 
        Then since \(P_\beta\in X\), \(P_\beta\) is a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\delta\).

        Assume first that \(\sup(D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\) where \(\theta = \aleph(P(\delta))\). Let \(Q\) be the least common refinement of \((P_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\). Note that \(|Q|\leq |P(\delta)|\). Since \(Q\) is well-orderable, 
        \(|Q| < \aleph(P(\delta))\). Since \(\sup(D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\), there is some \(A\in U\) such that \(|Q\restriction A| < \delta\).
        Thus there is then some \(\beta < \delta\) such that for all \(\beta'\geq \beta\), \(P_\beta\restriction A = P_{\beta'} \restriction A\). 
        Since \(P_\beta\in X\), \(P_\beta\) is a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\delta\).

        We finish by proving \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\delta\) in the case that \(\mathcal H\) does not correctly compute all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
        Take \(s\in \textnormal{Ord}^\omega\) such that
        \(\langle P_\beta : \beta < \delta\rangle\in \mathcal H_s\).
        Let \(\bar U = U\cap \mathcal H_s\) and let \(\mu = \sup (D(\bar U)\cap \delta)\). If \(\mu < \delta\), then since \(\delta\) is inaccessible in \(\mathcal H_s\), the sequence \((P_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\) stabilizes modulo \(U\): there cannot be a strictly Rudin--Keisler increasing \(\delta\)-sequence of measures on \(\mu\).

        So assume towards a contradiction that
        \(\mu = \delta\).
        Then some \(\kappa < \delta\) is \(\delta\)-supercompact 
        in \(\mathcal H_s\). 
        It follows from \cref{cor:ua_completeness} that \(\kappa_\delta < \delta\),
        and so by \cref{lma:hod_transfer}, \(\mathcal H\) correctly
        computes all finite successors of \(\delta^{++}\).
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- defn-12: body_md -->
Suppose \(D\) and \(U\) are measures and \(X\) is the underlying set of \(D\).
    Then \[s_U(D) =  \{A\in j_U(P(X)) : j_U^{-1}[A] \in D\}\]
<!-- end defn-12 -->
<!-- lma-12: body_md -->
\label{lma:inacc_to_internal}
    Suppose \(D\) and \(U\) are measures on ordinals,
    and \(U\) is not the
    \(D\)-limit of a sequence of measures Ketonen below \(U\).
    Then \(s_U(D)\in M_U\).
<!-- end lma-12 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
This is a choiceless analog of \cite[Lemma 5.5.15]{UABook}.
    The details are contained in the proofs of \cite[Lemma 3.19]{EvenOrdinals} and
    \cite[Theorem 3.8]{UAFromDet}.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
Assume \(\mathcal H\) does not correctly compute all finite
    successors of \(\lambda^{++}\). 
    Let \(F\) be a \(\lambda\)-complete filter on an ordinal
    \(\eta\). Let \(U\) be the Ketonen least measure on \(\eta\)
    extending \(F\). We claim that \(U\) is \(\lambda\)-complete.
    
    We first claim that \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\lambda\).
    If \(\lambda\) is an NT-cardinal, this is immediate from \cref{lma:NT_spectrum}. If \(\lambda\) is a limit of NT-cardinals,
    we first use \cref{lma:limit_spectrum} to conclude that
    \(\sup(D(U)\cap \lambda) < \lambda\). Fix an NT-cardinal
    \(\delta < \lambda\) above this supremum. Note now that
    \(\sup (D(U)\cap \theta) < \delta\) where \(\theta = \aleph(P(\delta))\).
    Therefore \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\delta\),
    and it follows easily that \(U\) has a finest partition below \(\lambda\).

    Next, we show that if \(D\) is a measure on a cardinal \(\gamma < \lambda\),
    then \(s_U(D) \in M_U\).
    In fact, if \(\vec U = \langle U_\xi : \xi < \gamma\rangle\) 
    is a sequence of measures on \(\eta\) and
    \(U_\xi<_{\Bbbk} U\) for all \(\xi < \gamma\), we show that 
    \(U^* = D\textnormal{-}\lim_{\xi < \gamma} U_\xi\) is not \(U\), and then cite \cref{thm:ua_N} and \cref{lma:inacc_to_internal}.
    Note that by the minimality of \(U\),
    \(F\setminus U_\xi\neq \emptyset\) for each \(\xi < \gamma\).
    We will find a single \(S\in F\setminus \bigcup_{\xi < \gamma} U_\xi\).

    For this, fix an NT-cardinal \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\) with
    \(\gamma < \delta \leq \lambda\). Take a sufficiently large ordinal \(\theta\), and find an elementary substructure 
    \(X\preceq V_{\theta+1}\) with \(|X| \leq^* |V_{\alpha+1}|\),
    containing \(V_{\alpha+1}\cup \{F,\vec U\}\). 
    Write \(X\) as a continuous increasing union of sets \((X_\beta)_{\beta < \delta}\) 
    where \(|X_\beta| \leq^* |V_\alpha|\). Fix \(\beta < \delta\) such that \(\gamma\cup \{F,\vec U\}\subseteq X_\beta\) and 
    \(X_\beta \preceq V_{\theta+1}\). Then for each \(\xi < \gamma\),
    \((F\setminus U_\xi)\cap X_\beta \neq \emptyset\).
    Let \(S = \bigcap (F\cap X_\beta)\). Clearly
    \(S\notin U_\xi\), but also \(S\in F\) since \(F\)
    is closed under \(V_\alpha\)-indexed intersections
    by \cite[Lemma 3.5]{Goldberg2024MeasurableChoiceless},
    using the fact that \(\aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \delta\)
    and \(F\) is \(\delta\)-complete.

    Thus
    \(s_U(D) \in M_U\) for every \(D\in \bigcup_{\gamma < \lambda}\textnormal{meas}(\gamma)\). 
    Let \(Q\) be a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\lambda\),
    with \(|Q|\) as small as possible. Let \(\gamma = |Q|\), and fix an NT-cardinal \(\delta\leq \lambda\) with \(\gamma < \delta\).

    Let \(D\) be the projection of \(U\) to a measure on \(Q\):
    \[D = \{A\subseteq Q: \bigcup A\in U\}\]
    We view \(D\) as a measure on \(\gamma\);
    then \(s_U(D) \in M_U\). Moreover by the minimality of \(|Q|\),
    \(D\) is uniform.

    To show \(U\) is \(\lambda\)-complete, it suffices to show that \(D\) is principal, so assume towards a contradiction that \(D\) is nonprincipal.
    Then since \(D\) is uniform on \(\gamma\) and \(D\leq_{\textnormal{RK}} U\), 
    \(j_U\) is discontinuous at \(\gamma\).
    Thus \(s_U(D)\) concentrates on
    \(\sup j_U[\gamma] < j_U(\gamma)\).
    
    Let \(s_U(D) = [f]_U\), where \(f : \eta \to \textnormal{meas}(\gamma)\)
    and each \(f(\xi)\) concentrates on some \(\nu < \gamma\).
    We claim that \(|\textnormal{ran}(f)| < \lambda\).
    If \(\lambda\) is a limit of NT-cardinals, this is
    easy since for all \(\gamma < \lambda\),
    fixing \(\alpha\) such that \(\gamma\leq \Theta(V_\alpha) < \lambda\), we have
    \(|\textnormal{ran}(f)| \leq |\textnormal{meas}(\gamma)| \leq \Theta(V_{\alpha+10}) < \lambda\). The more delicate case occurs when \(\lambda\) is an NT-cardinal.
    Then we claim that for each \(\nu < \gamma\), 
    \(|\textnormal{meas}(\nu)| < \lambda\). 
    Note here that
    \(\nu^+ < \lambda\), so by assumption \(\Theta(P(\nu))\leq \lambda\). 
    Fix \(\alpha\) such that \(\lambda = \Theta(V_\alpha)\).
    By the definition of an NT-cardinal,
    we can write \(P(\nu)\) as an increasing union \(\bigcup_{\beta < \lambda} Y_\beta\)
    where \(|Y_\beta|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\). 
    Since \(\Theta(P(\nu)) \leq \lambda\), we must have
    \(P(\nu)\subseteq Y_\beta\) for some \(\beta < \lambda\),
    which means that \(|P(\nu)|\leq^* |V_\alpha|\).
    Then \(|\textnormal{meas}(\nu)|\leq |V_{\alpha+1}|\), and thus
    \(|\textnormal{meas}(\nu)| < \aleph(V_{\alpha+1}) = \lambda\).
    Since \(\lambda\) is regular by \cref{lma:nt_inacc}, 
    it follows that the set of measures on \(\gamma\) that concentrate on an ordinal \(\nu < \gamma\) has cardinality
    less than \(\lambda\). Hence \(|\textnormal{ran}(f)| < \lambda\), as claimed.

    Since \(|\textnormal{ran}(f)| < \lambda\) and \(Q\) is a finest partition of \(U\) below \(\lambda\), letting \(k : M_D\to M_U\) be the factor embedding, we have \(s_U(D) = [f]_U\in \textnormal{ran}(k)\).
    It follows that \[s_D(D) = k^{-1}[s_U(D)]\in M_D\]
    Since \(D\) is nonprincipal, \(s_D(D)\in M_D\) yields a
    strict Lipschitz reduction from \(D\) to itself:
    let \(\langle D_\alpha : \alpha < \gamma\rangle\) represent
    \(s_D(D)\), and note that \(\alpha\in D_\alpha\) for all \(\alpha < \gamma\)
    since \(D\) is uniform and \(s_D(D)\) concentrates on \(\sup j_D[\gamma]\);
    we have \(S\in D\) if and only if \(\{\alpha < \gamma : S\cap \alpha\in D_\alpha\}\in D\).
    This contradicts
    \cite[Corollary 3.4.29]{UABook}.

    Thus we have shown that \(D\) is principal, and hence \(U\) is \(\lambda\)-complete.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- thm-19: body_md -->
\label{thm:filter_extension}
    In \(N\), suppose \(\alpha\geq \omega\) is not of the form \(\beta+1\) where \(\beta > \omega\) and \(\textnormal{cf}(\beta) = \omega\).
    Let \(\delta = \Theta(V_\alpha)\). Then every
    \(\delta\)-complete filter on an ordinal extends to a
    \(\delta\)-complete ultrafilter.
    \begin{proof}
        If \(\alpha = \omega\), then \(\Theta(V_\alpha) =\omega_1\), and we obtain the filter extension property from Kunen's theorem.
        If \(\alpha\) is a successor ordinal, then 
        \(\text{NT}_\text{base}\) combined with \cref{cor:no-long-rank-sequences} yields that \(\delta\) is an NT-cardinal, so we can apply \cref{thm:NT-filter-extension}. 
        If \(\alpha\) is a limit ordinal,
        then by \cref{lma:limit-successor-regular} of \cref{thm:theta_meas}, \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = \lambda^+\)
        where \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_{\beta})\).
        Then \(\lambda\) is a limit of NT-cardinals, so by \cref{thm:NT-filter-extension}, every \(\lambda\)-complete filter on an ordinal extends to a \(\lambda\)-complete ultrafilter.
        By minimality, \(\lambda\) is singular, and therefore
        \(\lambda\)-complete filters are \(\lambda^+\)-complete. This yields the conclusion.
    \end{proof}
<!-- end thm-19 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
If \(\alpha = \omega\), then \(\Theta(V_\alpha) =\omega_1\), and we obtain the filter extension property from Kunen's theorem.
        If \(\alpha\) is a successor ordinal, then 
        \(\text{NT}_\text{base}\) combined with \cref{cor:no-long-rank-sequences} yields that \(\delta\) is an NT-cardinal, so we can apply \cref{thm:NT-filter-extension}. 
        If \(\alpha\) is a limit ordinal,
        then by \cref{lma:limit-successor-regular} of \cref{thm:theta_meas}, \(\Theta(V_\alpha) = \lambda^+\)
        where \(\lambda = \sup_{\beta < \alpha} \Theta(V_{\beta})\).
        Then \(\lambda\) is a limit of NT-cardinals, so by \cref{thm:NT-filter-extension}, every \(\lambda\)-complete filter on an ordinal extends to a \(\lambda\)-complete ultrafilter.
        By minimality, \(\lambda\) is singular, and therefore
        \(\lambda\)-complete filters are \(\lambda^+\)-complete. This yields the conclusion.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
